<!-- Service cards -->
<h3 class="subsec">Service Specifications</h3>

<div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12px;margin:0 0 12px">
 <div style="flex:1;height:2px;background:linear-gradient(to right,#5A2D82,transparent)"></div>
 <span style="font-size:10px;font-weight:800;color:#5A2D82;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.14em;white-space:nowrap">themis-research</span>
 <div style="flex:1;height:2px;background:linear-gradient(to left,#5A2D82,transparent)"></div>
</div>
<p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);margin-bottom:14px">Active research orchestration, historical outcome patterns, requirement similarity matching, anticipatory surfacing.</p>

<!-- SCRIBE -->
<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#1B3A5C;margin-bottom:16px">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#EEF2F8;color:#1B3A5C">Year 2 · Q1–Q2</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#EAF0F8;color:#2C5F8A">themis-knowledge</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#1B3A5C">SCRIBE <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Document Version & Semantic Diff Intelligence</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#1B3A5C">The Recorder — keeper of what changed and why</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">Tracks substantive changes across document versions with AI-assisted semantic understanding. Distinguishes edits that changed intelligence significance from edits that changed style, flags provisions that disappeared without explanation, and identifies where AI-generated content in earlier versions was silently modified in later ones. Not track-changes — semantic diff with intelligence significance awareness, integrated with the THEMIS provenance graph.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Core Capabilities</div>
   <ul style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.6">
    <li><strong>Semantic diff engine</strong> — Classifies each change: substantive (alters intelligence significance), structural (reorganizes), or stylistic (phrasing only)</li>
    <li><strong>AI contribution tracking</strong> — Shows which clauses contain AI-generated content and whether changes moved toward or away from the AI's original output</li>
    <li><strong>Provision disappearance detection</strong> — Flags provisions absent in a later version without a corresponding replacement or deletion note</li>
    <li><strong>Cross-matter clause library</strong> — Queryable library of clause variations across matters with TVS validity scoring</li>
    <li><strong>Version validity inheritance</strong> — Tracks how validity scores change across versions and which changes caused declines</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Integration</div>
   <div class="svc-io"><strong>Depends on:</strong> Provenance/MOIRAI · TVS/KAIROS · ERAS/LOGOS · FGTS/ALETHEIA (corpus)</div>
   <div class="svc-io" style="margin-top:6px"><strong>Feeds into:</strong> MNEMOSYNE · STOA · Client attestation</div>
   <div style="margin-top:10px;font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">Reads from MOIRAI's provenance graph to identify AI-contributed content per version. Writes VersionDiff records back as edges between document node versions. TVS validity scores inform the semantic diff classifier — a clause change referencing a low-validity source is flagged differently.</div>
  </div>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- STOA -->
<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#0D6B5E;margin-bottom:16px">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#E6F5F3;color:#0D6B5E">Year 2 · Q1–Q2</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F0EBF9;color:#5A2D82">themis-research</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#0D6B5E">STOA <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Intelligence Research Orchestration</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#0D6B5E">The Forum — where structured analytic reasoning is assembled</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">Structures intelligence research as documented multi-step workflows. Where a single RAG call retrieves and synthesizes, STOA orchestrates: query decomposition, iterative refinement, sub-question routing, source triangulation, and synthesis — with a complete research trail showing every step from initial question to final answer. ERAS captures the reasoning; STOA captures the methodology. The distinction matters when an analyst must explain how they arrived at a legal position.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Core Capabilities</div>
   <ul style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.6">
    <li><strong>Query decomposition</strong> — Breaks intelligence research into sub-questions across five types: threshold, factual, precedential, jurisdiction, current-law</li>
    <li><strong>Q-RAG integration</strong> — Multi-step retrieval for each sub-question with full retrieval trajectory logging</li>
    <li><strong>Source triangulation</strong> — Cross-references sources across sub-questions, flags TVS conflicts between sources retrieved for different sub-questions</li>
    <li><strong>Research trail documentation</strong> — Structured memo: question tree, sources per sub-question, TVS validity scores at research time, synthesis logic</li>
    <li><strong>Iterative refinement</strong> — Multi-turn research sessions with refinement logic logged for full reconstructability</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Integration</div>
   <div class="svc-io"><strong>Depends on:</strong> RQS/HERMES (Q-RAG) · Provenance/MOIRAI · TVS/KAIROS · ERAS/LOGOS · KCS/ARGUS</div>
   <div class="svc-io" style="margin-top:6px"><strong>Feeds into:</strong> MNEMOSYNE · PYTHIA · SCRIBE · Client research trail attestation</div>
   <div style="margin-top:10px;font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">Every STOA research session produces MOIRAI provenance records — the research trail is a first-class provenance artifact. KCS consulted on whether retrieved authority has been affected by recent external developments before synthesis is delivered.</div>
  </div>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- ORACLE -->
<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#9A6800;margin-bottom:16px">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#FFF7E8;color:#9A6800">Year 2 · Q3–Q4</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F0EBF9;color:#5A2D82">themis-research</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#9A6800">ORACLE <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Matter Outcome Intelligence</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#9A6800">The Foreseer — patterns that predict what comes next</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">Surfaces predictive intelligence from the agency's historical matter outcomes. Every pattern is transparent, attributable, and traceable to specific historical matters — not a black box model score but an auditable signal. "Three prior requirements with this target type were assessed within 45 days of collection initiation at 70% confidence accuracy" is the kind of intelligence ORACLE produces. Requires 200+ historical matters for statistical significance.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Core Capabilities</div>
   <ul style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.6">
    <li><strong>Outcome pattern modeling</strong> — Assessment closure timing, collection success rates, analytic conclusion accuracy stratified by requirement type, target type, domain, and collection approach</li>
    <li><strong>Adversary and target profiling</strong> — Structured profiles: target behavior patterns, collection responsiveness, historical deception indicators, denial and deception signature</li>
    <li><strong>Decision-maker and command analytics</strong> — Policymaker response patterns, tasking tendencies, collection priority shifts, historical use of finished intelligence</li>
    <li><strong>Timeline and resource modeling</strong> — Expected collection phases, resource requirements, assessment timelines based on actual agency experience</li>
    <li><strong>Transparent signal attribution</strong> — Every insight shows the underlying requirements driving it — analysts can evaluate comparability themselves</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Integration</div>
   <div class="svc-io"><strong>Depends on:</strong> Provenance/MOIRAI · FGTS/ALETHEIA · TCS/MIMIR · KCS/ARGUS · MIRROR</div>
   <div class="svc-io" style="margin-top:6px"><strong>Feeds into:</strong> MNEMOSYNE · PYTHIA · Client outcome intelligence · INTELLECT</div>
   <div style="margin-top:10px;font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">FGTS correction patterns inform which ORACLE predictions were trusted vs. overridden by analysts — providing a feedback signal that improves model accuracy. TCS calibration data identifies analysts systematically over- or under-weighting ORACLE signals.</div>
  </div>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- MIRROR -->
<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#4A2FA8;margin-bottom:16px">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#EEEBFF;color:#4A2FA8">Year 2 · Q3</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F0EBF9;color:#5A2D82">themis-research</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#4A2FA8">MIRROR <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Similar Matter Intelligence</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#4A2FA8">The Reflector — shows you where you have been before</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">Identifies the most similar historical matters in the agency's corpus for any active requirement — by analytic approach, target domain, adversary profile, geopolitical context, and collection pattern. Shows what worked, what didn't, and what unexpected complications arose. Makes institutional experience searchable and comparable rather than dependent on whether the right partner happens to be available to take a call. Improves continuously from a minimum of 50 matters.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Core Capabilities</div>
   <ul style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.6">
    <li><strong>Multi-dimensional similarity</strong> — Six dimensions: collection approach, target domain, adversary profile, geopolitical context, source pattern, geographic focus. Each dimension weighted by requirement type.</li>
    <li><strong>Outcome-linked comparison</strong> — Similar requirements shown with outcomes: assessment accuracy, collection timeline, resource consumption, decisive analytic moves</li>
    <li><strong>Complication pattern surfacing</strong> — Collection gaps, source reliability issues, denial and deception patterns that arose in similar requirements but are not yet apparent in the active requirement</li>
    <li><strong>Analyst experience matching</strong> — Maps which analysts have actual prior experience in the most similar matters for informed staffing</li>
    <li><strong>Continuous similarity refresh</strong> — Re-runs similarity scoring as the active requirement's profile evolves with new collection and analytic findings</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Integration</div>
   <div class="svc-io"><strong>Depends on:</strong> Provenance/MOIRAI · RQS/HERMES · TVS/KAIROS · STOA</div>
   <div class="svc-io" style="margin-top:6px"><strong>Feeds into:</strong> ORACLE · MNEMOSYNE · PYTHIA · INTELLECT</div>
   <div style="margin-top:10px;font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">MIRROR's similarity engine uses embeddings from the RQS vector store. MOIRAI provenance records provide full assessment history including AI interaction patterns. TVS validity scores on historical matter documents inform comparison quality — a similar matter with low-validity documents is a weaker comparable.</div>
  </div>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- MNEMOSYNE -->
<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#157A6B;margin-bottom:16px">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#E6F5F3;color:#157A6B">Year 3 · Q1</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#EAF0F8;color:#2C5F8A">themis-knowledge</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#157A6B">MNEMOSYNE <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Institutional Memory Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#157A6B">The Memory — mother of all the Muses; keeper of what was known</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">Captures, structures, and makes queryable the tacit institutional knowledge that currently exists only in the minds of experienced analysts — and walks out the door when they leave. Aggregates signal from FGTS correction patterns, ORACLE outcome analysis, MIRROR similarity intelligence, and STOA research trails to build a structured, searchable institutional memory. Surfaces that knowledge when and where it is relevant, without requiring analysts to know it exists. Cannot be built until FGTS corpus is mature and ORACLE and MIRROR are operational.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Core Capabilities</div>
   <ul style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.6">
    <li><strong>Tacit knowledge extraction</strong> — Mines FGTS correction patterns for implicit knowledge — systematic analyst overrides encode what experienced analysts know that the model doesn't</li>
    <li><strong>Knowledge graph construction</strong> — Requirement types, analytic frameworks, successful collection strategies, adversary behavior, command tendencies, source performance — all attributable to source data</li>
    <li><strong>Expertise locator</strong> — Maps analyst expertise to specific knowledge domains from their assessment history and FGTS correction patterns, not just stated practice area</li>
    <li><strong>Contextual knowledge surfacing</strong> — Pushes relevant knowledge into active requirements without requiring analysts to search — receives when context indicates applicability</li>
    <li><strong>Knowledge decay tracking</strong> — TVS-style decay modeling applied to institutional knowledge records — flags stale knowledge, prioritizes fresh signals</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Integration</div>
   <div class="svc-io"><strong>Depends on:</strong> FGTS/ALETHEIA · ORACLE · MIRROR · STOA · Provenance/MOIRAI · TVS/KAIROS</div>
   <div class="svc-io" style="margin-top:6px"><strong>Feeds into:</strong> PYTHIA · INTELLECT · Client expertise attestation</div>
   <div style="margin-top:10px;font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">MNEMOSYNE is the synthesis layer above FGTS, ORACLE, and MIRROR — it aggregates rather than generates. Writes its knowledge graph to INTELLECT as the primary institutional intelligence surface.</div>
  </div>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- PYTHIA -->
<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#8B2222;margin-bottom:28px">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#FBF0F0;color:#8B2222">Year 3 · Q2+</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F0EBF9;color:#5A2D82">themis-research</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#8B2222">PYTHIA <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Predictive Research Intelligence</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#8B2222">The Prophet — surfaces what you need before you ask</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">Anticipates what research, evidence, and intelligence a analytic team will need before they know to ask for it. As a matter progresses through its lifecycle — collection initiation, source development, analytic production, policymaker delivery — PYTHIA surfaces the research and evidence most likely to be needed at each stage, based on MIRROR's similar matter set and MNEMOSYNE's institutional knowledge about how those matters developed. The difference between an analyst pulling information when asked and a system that anticipates what you need next.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Core Capabilities</div>
   <ul style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.6">
    <li><strong>Lifecycle stage modeling</strong> — Identifies current collection phase, models what typically happens next based on MIRROR's similar requirement set, produces forward-looking intelligence brief</li>
    <li><strong>Proactive research surfacing</strong> — Pre-executes via STOA the research most likely to be relevant based on stage and profile — results are ready when the analyst needs them</li>
    <li><strong>Evidence gap detection</strong> — Compares current matter evidence corpus against what similar matters had assembled at the same stage, surfaces missing evidence categories</li>
    <li><strong>Deadline anticipation</strong> — Surfaces upcoming deadlines with the lead time MIRROR and ORACLE suggest is needed for the specific work required at this stage</li>
    <li><strong>Requirement intelligence brief</strong> — Daily or weekly synthesis: external changes (KCS), source validity status (TVS), ORACLE outcome trajectory, PYTHIA next recommendations</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--muted);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.1em;margin-bottom:6px">Integration</div>
   <div class="svc-io"><strong>Depends on:</strong> MIRROR · MNEMOSYNE · STOA · ORACLE · KCS/ARGUS · TVS/KAIROS</div>
   <div class="svc-io" style="margin-top:6px"><strong>Feeds into:</strong> Analyst tool matter sideoversight · INTELLECT matter intelligence feed · Client</div>
   <div style="margin-top:10px;font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">PYTHIA is the most downstream service — requires nearly everything else operational to deliver full value. The matter intelligence brief it produces is the primary surface through which all intelligence services speak to the analyst in a unified, actionable format.</div>
  </div>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- Intelligence Flywheel -->
<h3 class="subsec">The Intelligence Flywheel</h3>
<div style="background:#F4F7FB;border-radius:10px;padding:24px 28px;margin-bottom:20px;border:1px solid var(--border)">
 <div style="display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr 1fr;gap:0;position:relative">
  <div style="position:absolute;top:50%;left:0;right:0;height:2px;background:rgba(255,255,255,.15)"></div>
  <div style="text-align:center;padding:0 12px">
   <div style="background:#EEF2F8;border-radius:8px;padding:14px;margin-bottom:8px;border:1px solid #C0CEDF">
    <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:#1B3A5C;margin-bottom:4px">SCRIBE + STOA</div>
    <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--sub)">Document & research intelligence. Extends provenance and retrieval infrastructure.</div>
   </div>
   <div style="font-size:10px;color:var(--muted);font-weight:600;letter-spacing:.1em">YEAR 2 Q1–Q2</div>
  </div>
  <div style="text-align:center;padding:0 12px">
   <div style="background:#EEEAFF;border-radius:8px;padding:14px;margin-bottom:8px;border:1px solid #C4B5FD">
    <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:#4A2FA8;margin-bottom:4px">MIRROR + ORACLE</div>
    <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--sub)">Matter intelligence. Requires 50+ matters (MIRROR), 200+ matters (ORACLE).</div>
   </div>
   <div style="font-size:10px;color:var(--muted);font-weight:600;letter-spacing:.1em">YEAR 2 Q3–Q4</div>
  </div>
  <div style="text-align:center;padding:0 12px">
   <div style="background:#E6F5F3;border-radius:8px;padding:14px;margin-bottom:8px;border:1px solid #A0D0C4">
    <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:#0D6B5E;margin-bottom:4px">MNEMOSYNE + PYTHIA</div>
    <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--sub)">Institutional intelligence. Aggregates from all prior services. Full value requires everything upstream.</div>
   </div>
   <div style="font-size:10px;color:var(--muted);font-weight:600;letter-spacing:.1em">YEAR 3</div>
  </div>
 </div>
 <div style="margin-top:20px;padding-top:16px;border-top:1px solid var(--border)">
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.7;text-align:center">Every matter that flows through the platform adds to the intelligence layer. FGTS accumulates corrections. MIRROR adds a comparable. ORACLE refines its outcome models. MNEMOSYNE extracts new institutional knowledge. PYTHIA becomes more accurate. The flywheel is self-reinforcing — and it is built on the agency's own data, which no external vendor can replicate.</p>
 </div>
</div>


<!-- themis-knowledge namespace -->
<!-- OGS/YGGDRASIL service card -->
<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#2C5F8A;margin-bottom:16px">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#E8F2FB;color:#2C5F8A">Year 2 · Q1 · Semantic Foundation</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#2C5F8A">OGS / YGGDRASIL <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Ontology Governance Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#2C5F8A">The World Tree — the semantic structure that connects all intelligence domains</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">Maintains the domain ontology for intelligence analysis: a machine-readable, version-controlled graph of canonical entity types (target, source, program, indicator, collection operation, assessment, policymaker), their properties, and their valid relationship types. OGS/YGGDRASIL is not an intelligence service — it is the semantic foundation that intelligence services depend on. Without it, MNEMOSYNE's knowledge graph is pattern-matched text without genuine semantic grounding. With it, STOA, ORACLE, MIRROR, and PYTHIA can reason about entities and relationships with shared semantics rather than guessing at meaning.</div>

 <div class="card" style="background:#F0F6FC;border:1px solid #C8DCF0;padding:10px 12px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#2C5F8A;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:6px">Why an ontology is required for agentic intelligence services</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">Traditional analytics tolerated semantic ambiguity because humans resolved it. Agentic services — STOA orchestrating multi-step research, PYTHIA surfacing intelligence before analysts ask — operate without continuous human oversight on individual inference steps. When they encounter "target," "actor," and "entity of interest" used across SIGINT, HUMINT, and IMINT reports, they need a shared semantic framework to know these terms refer to the same underlying concept type. Without one, they confidently reason about relationships that are semantically incoherent. The ontology is the shared rulebook that makes agentic reasoning about intelligence entities reliable.</p>
 </div>

 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:12px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Core Capabilities</h5>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.7">
    <li><strong>Canonical entity model</strong> — Machine-readable definitions of intelligence domain entity types and their properties. Version-controlled with IOB approval for changes.</li>
    <li><strong>Relationship graph</strong> — Defines which entity types can have which relationships. Constrains AI assertions to semantically valid relationship claims.</li>
    <li><strong>Cross-INT entity resolution</strong> — Recognizes that three reports from SIGINT, HUMINT, and IMINT using different vocabularies are discussing the same underlying entity.</li>
    <li><strong>Ontology governance API</strong> — Exposes the ontology to all intelligence layer services at inference time, ensuring consistent semantic interpretation across the platform.</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Naming: YGGDRASIL</h5>
   <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin-bottom:8px">In Norse mythology, Yggdrasil is the world tree — the great ash that connects all nine worlds, holding the entire cosmology together through shared structure. It is the appropriate name for an ontology service: the semantic structure that connects all intelligence domains, collection streams, and institutional systems into a coherent whole that machines can traverse.</p>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Existing IC Standards</h5>
   <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6">OGS/YGGDRASIL is built on existing IC ontological standards (STIX for threat entities, NSG for geospatial entities, applicable agency taxonomies) rather than authoring a new IC ontology from scratch. Its job is to make existing standards machine-readable and available to THEMIS intelligence layer services, with a governance mechanism for domain-specific extensions.</p>
  </div>
 </div>

 <table class="decision-table" style="margin-bottom:8px">
  <tr><th>Downstream service</th><th>What OGS/YGGDRASIL enables</th></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>MNEMOSYNE</strong></td><td>Knowledge graph edges become semantically valid relationships, not pattern-matched text. Institutional memory becomes queryable by entity type and relationship class.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>STOA</strong></td><td>Multi-step research orchestration can correctly map entities across INT types during query decomposition and source triangulation.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>ORACLE / MIRROR</strong></td><td>Historical requirement matching and outcome modeling operate on semantically grounded entity comparisons rather than surface text similarity.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>PYTHIA</strong></td><td>Anticipatory surfacing is more precise when entity and relationship semantics are grounded — fewer false-positive recommendations based on superficial lexical similarity.</td></tr>
  <tr><td><strong>CVS / VERITAS</strong></td><td>Source corroboration becomes entity-aware — the same entity referenced with different terminology across sources can be correctly identified as the same entity.</td></tr>
 </table>
</div>

<div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12px;margin:28px 0 12px">
 <div style="flex:1;height:2px;background:linear-gradient(to right,#8A2E2E,transparent)"></div>
 <span style="font-size:10px;font-weight:800;color:#8A2E2E;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.14em;white-space:nowrap">themis-warning</span>
 <div style="flex:1;height:2px;background:linear-gradient(to left,#8A2E2E,transparent)"></div>
</div>
<p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);margin-bottom:14px">Temporal reasoning, behavioral anomaly detection, collection gap mapping, weak signal fusion, cross-requirement correlation, strategic warning synthesis. Year 3–4.</p>

<!-- TRS/CHRONOS -->
<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#8A4C2E;margin-bottom:16px">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#FBF3EE;color:#8A4C2E">Year 3 · Q1 · Intelligence Layer · Temporal Reasoning</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F9EAEA;color:#8A2E2E">themis-warning</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#8A4C2E">TRS / CHRONOS <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Temporal Reasoning Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#8A4C2E">Not the deity who controls time — time itself as an active shaping force</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">Addresses temporal forecasting as a distinct analytical mode. Forecasting what an adversary will do next, how a capability is evolving, when a threshold event will occur — this is analytically and epistemically different from reasoning about current states. Current-state assessments describe something that has a determinate value (even if unknown). Forecasts describe something that may not yet exist — the adversary may not have decided, the outcome may depend on conditions not yet materialized. Collapsing both into the same confidence framework systematically misleads analysts and decision-makers about the nature of their uncertainty.</div>

 <div class="card" style="background:#FBF3EE;border:1px solid #D4A882;padding:10px 12px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#8A4C2E;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:6px">Why temporal forecasting requires its own service</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">A forecast that said a 30% probability event would not happen, and then it happened, is not a failed forecast. A current-state assessment that said the capability did not exist, when it did, is a failed assessment. These require different accuracy measurement, different outcome feedback, different calibration. TRS/CHRONOS produces outputs that are explicitly labeled as temporal projections — not current-state assessments — and routes them through a distinct UCS/TYCHE temporal uncertainty profile before any analyst or policymaker sees them.</p>
 </div>

 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:12px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Core Capabilities</h5>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.7">
    <li><strong>Trajectory modeling</strong> — time-indexed probability distributions over key indicators, derived from ORACLE outcomes, MNEMOSYNE adversary behavioral patterns, and CASSANDRA anomaly outputs. Labeled as projections with confidence intervals, never as predictions.</li>
    <li><strong>Leading indicator monitoring</strong> — observable collection signatures that historically precede threshold events. Alerts fire when current collection matches or deviates from a pattern, with historical confirmation rate and typical lead time.</li>
    <li><strong>Scenario space generation</strong> — for high-priority requirements: structured plausible future states, probability distributions, materialization conditions, discriminating indicators. Feeds PCS/IRIS consumer packages and ATHENA analyst framing.</li>
    <li><strong>SENTINEL coupling</strong> — trajectories diverging from historical patterns feed SWS/SENTINEL as strategic warning precursors. Absence of expected leading indicators feeds ADS/CASSANDRA.</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Uncertainty Profile</h5>
   <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:8px">All TRS outputs pass through UCS/TYCHE with an explicit <strong>temporal uncertainty component</strong>: horizon length (longer = higher uncertainty), whether aleatory is high (subject hasn't decided) or low (trajectory constrained by physical limits), sensitivity to key assumptions.</p>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Dependencies</h5>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.7">
    <li>OFS/NEMESIS — outcome data for trajectory validation</li>
    <li>UCS/TYCHE — temporal uncertainty decomposition</li>
    <li>ORACLE + MNEMOSYNE — behavioral baselines</li>
    <li>ADS/CASSANDRA — anomaly precursors</li>
    <li>SWS/SENTINEL — strategic warning integration</li>
   </ul>
   <div style="background:#FBF0F0;border:1px solid #E8B4B4;border-radius:5px;padding:8px 10px;margin-top:8px;font-size:11px;color:#6B1818">
    <strong>GC-8 required:</strong> Forecast product governance — labeling standards, accuracy measurement for probabilistic assertions, outcome feedback framework — must be designed before production deployment.
   </div>
  </div>
 </div>

 <table class="decision-table">
  <tr><th>Output type</th><th>Consumer</th><th>Uncertainty treatment</th></tr>
  <tr><td>Trajectory model</td><td>Analyst (ATHENA session framing)</td><td>Probability distribution with confidence intervals. Labeled as projection.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Leading indicator alert</td><td>Analyst + supervisor</td><td>Historical confirmation rate surfaced. Absence-of-indicator routed to CASSANDRA.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Scenario space</td><td>Analyst + PCS/IRIS → policymaker</td><td>Full UCS/TYCHE temporal profile. Discriminating indicators per scenario.</td></tr>
  <tr><td>Strategic warning precursor</td><td>SWS/SENTINEL</td><td>SENTINEL applies own threshold before surfacing to supervisors.</td></tr>
 </table>
</div>


<!-- Intelligence Cycle Completion services -->
<h3 class="subsec" style="margin-top:32px">Intelligence Cycle Completion <span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:400;color:var(--muted);margin-left:8px">Addendum F · Year 2 Q3–Q4 · Closing the downstream and upstream loops</span></h3>
<p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:16px">The intelligence layer as designed reasons about the analysis phase. These two services extend THEMIS upstream to collection requirements and downstream to policymaker delivery — completing the intelligence cycle loops that Phase 1–8 governance leaves open.</p>

<div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:16px;margin-bottom:20px">

<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#8A4C2E">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#FBF3EE;color:#8A4C2E">Year 2 · Q3 · themis-dissemination · Addendum F</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#8A4C2E">PCS / IRIS <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Policymaker Communication Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#8A4C2E">The Messenger — translating between analytical knowledge and decision-maker action</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">ATHENA is designed for analysts. Intelligence products from ATHENA go to policymakers who do not understand calibrated confidence, source type badges, or provenance chains. The accountability chain ends at dissemination. Everything that happens after — how the policymaker interprets the assessment, what weight they give it, how they act on it — is outside the platform's governance. PCS/IRIS closes that gap by translating the technical provenance and calibration metadata into a consumer package that decision-makers can read, interpret, and act on without understanding the platform.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Consumer Package Contents</h5>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.7">
    <li><strong>Assessment basis summary</strong> — plain-language source characteristics (not names), collection recency, currency status</li>
    <li><strong>Confidence translation</strong> — three sentences from UCS/TYCHE decomposition: what we assess, what kind of uncertainty, what would change it</li>
    <li><strong>Key assumptions</strong> — analytical assumptions the assessment depends on, from Intention Gate records in MOIRAI</li>
    <li><strong>Falsification indicators</strong> — observable events that would call the assessment into question. The single most important element: transforms an assertion into a testable hypothesis</li>
    <li><strong>Prior outcome record</strong> — plain-language accuracy summary from ORACLE/MIRROR for similar assessments</li>
    <li><strong>Provenance certificate</strong> — cryptographically attested summary readable to non-technical audiences</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Dependencies</h5>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.7">
    <li>UCS/TYCHE — decomposed uncertainty profile for the three-sentence confidence translation</li>
    <li>MOIRAI — Intention Gate records for key assumptions</li>
    <li>DPS/CODEX — document provenance records</li>
    <li>ORACLE/MIRROR — prior accuracy data for similar assessments</li>
   </ul>
   <div style="background:#FBF0F0;border:1px solid #E8B4B4;border-radius:5px;padding:8px 10px;margin-top:8px;font-size:11px;color:#6B1818">
    <strong>GC-6 required:</strong> Consumer package content policy — what appears in the package, classification relative to underlying product, modification authority before delivery.
   </div>
  </div>
 </div>
 <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);border-top:1px solid var(--border);padding-top:8px"><strong>Naming:</strong> IRIS is the goddess of the rainbow — the messenger who traveled between Olympus and the mortal world, translating between divine knowledge and mortal action. PCS/IRIS translates between the analytical platform and the decision-makers it serves.</div>
</div>

<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#1E6B3A">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#E6F5EE;color:#1E6B3A">Year 2 · Q4 · themis-requirements · Addendum F</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#1E6B3A">TIS / DIKE <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Tasking Integration Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#1E6B3A">Justice as proper order — things in their correct sequence in the intelligence cycle</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">The intelligence cycle begins with collection requirements. Someone determines that intelligence on a topic is needed, tasks collection assets, and the resulting intelligence eventually reaches the analyst. THEMIS currently enters the cycle only after collection, processing, and ingestion. The gaps ARGUS-LACUNA identifies have no path upstream — they accumulate as known gaps but cannot trigger collection requirements without a human manually translating. TIS/DIKE closes the upstream loop by connecting THEMIS to the requirements management infrastructure.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Core Capabilities</h5>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:16px;line-height:1.7">
    <li><strong>Gap-to-requirement pipeline</strong> — when ARGUS-LACUNA identifies a collection gap, TIS generates a Collection Gap Request routed to requirements officers with: the specific uncovered domain, estimated uncertainty reduction if closed (from UCS/TYCHE), and suggested collection approach from ORACLE outcome data</li>
    <li><strong>Collection effectiveness feedback</strong> — ORACLE outcome data returned to requirements officers in requirements-native format: what collection profiles were associated with accurate assessments, how this requirement compares to similar historical ones</li>
    <li><strong>Requirements lifecycle tracking</strong> — full lifecycle from initial tasking through collection, analysis, dissemination, and outcome. Basis for ORACLE predictive modeling and requirements-level provenance reports</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <h5 style="font-size:11.5px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Integration model</h5>
   <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:8px">TIS is an integration layer, not a requirements management replacement. Existing requirements management systems remain authoritative. TIS consumes their data, enriches it with THEMIS analytical context, and returns gap signals and effectiveness data.</p>
   <div style="background:#E6F5EE;border:1px solid #A8D4B8;border-radius:5px;padding:8px 10px;font-size:11px;color:#1E6B3A;margin-bottom:8px"><strong>Prerequisite:</strong> API agreements with requirements management system owners must be in place before Phase 2 Q4 deployment.</div>
   <div style="background:#FBF0F0;border:1px solid #E8B4B4;border-radius:5px;padding:8px 10px;font-size:11px;color:#6B1818"><strong>GC-7 required:</strong> Partner registry governance (CLS/PROTEUS) and requirements authority definitions before TIS deployment.</div>
  </div>
 </div>
 <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);border-top:1px solid var(--border);padding-top:8px"><strong>Naming:</strong> DIKE is the Greek goddess of justice and moral order — specifically the justice that comes from things being in their proper place and sequence. The intelligence cycle has a proper sequence; DIKE restores it.</div>
</div>

</div>

<!-- Unknown Unknown services -->
<h3 class="subsec" style="margin-top:32px">Unknown Unknown Services <span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:400;color:var(--muted);margin-left:8px">Proposed — Year 3–4 · Rumsfeld Layer</span></h3>
<div style="background:#F5F0FF;border:2px dashed #9B7FD4;border-radius:8px;padding:12px 16px;margin-bottom:20px;font-size:12.5px;color:#3A1A70;line-height:1.65">
 <strong>Design rationale:</strong> THEMIS as designed is primarily a <em>known unknown</em> architecture — it answers questions the analyst knows to ask. These five services address what we don't know we don't know: anomalies in behavioral baselines, gaps in collection coverage, weak signals below individual attention thresholds, cross-requirement correlations, and strategic surprise indicators. They do not eliminate unknown unknowns. They convert more of them into known unknowns faster than an ungoverned platform would.
</div>

<div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:16px;margin-bottom:20px">

<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#5A2D82">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F0EBF9;color:#5A2D82">Year 3 · Q3 · Proposed</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F9EAEA;color:#8A2E2E">themis-warning</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#5A2D82">ADS / CASSANDRA <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Anomaly Detection Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#5A2D82">The Prophet of Unheard Warnings — surfaces what should be there but isn't</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:10px">Watches the full collection picture for deviations from established behavioral baselines. If an adversary program typically generates signature X at development stage Y, and we are at stage Y with no signature X in the collection picture, CASSANDRA surfaces that absence as an analytical signal. Works on the premise that anomaly — deviation from known pattern — is itself intelligence, not noise.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:10px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#5A2D82;margin-bottom:5px">Dependencies</div>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.7;margin:0">
    <li>ORACLE — behavioral baselines from historical requirement outcomes</li>
    <li>MNEMOSYNE — adversary behavioral signature corpus</li>
    <li>KCS/ARGUS — current collection picture for comparison</li>
    <li>TVS/KAIROS — validity-weighted sources only in baseline</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;margin-bottom:5px">Design constraints</div>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.7;margin:0">
    <li>Requires ORACLE maturity — minimum 200 requirements with outcome data</li>
    <li>False positive rates will be high in early deployment</li>
    <li>Advisory signals only until false positive rate characterized</li>
    <li>Distinguishing meaningful anomaly from noise requires domain-encoded baseline</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
 </div>
 <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);border-top:1px solid var(--border);padding-top:8px"><strong>Naming:</strong> CASSANDRA warned Troy of its destruction and was not believed. The service surfaces warnings that are structurally likely to be ignored — absence signals are cognitively harder to act on than presence signals. The name is a design reminder to take absence anomalies seriously.</div>
</div>

<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#5A2D82">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F0EBF9;color:#5A2D82">Year 3 · Q4 · Proposed</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F9EAEA;color:#8A2E2E">themis-warning</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#5A2D82">CGS / ARGUS-LACUNA <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Collection Gap Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#5A2D82">The Blind Spots — making the shape of the unknown visible</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:10px">For any active requirement, maps what collection coverage exists and what is absent. Distinguishes two critically different types of silence: absence-of-evidence (collection is pointed at the target and finding nothing) versus absence-of-collection (no collection asset is pointed at this domain at all). An analyst receiving PARAM claims may not know whether the AI's silence reflects the target's absence or the platform's blindness.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:10px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#5A2D82;margin-bottom:5px">Core capability</div>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.7;margin:0">
    <li>Collection coverage map by domain and target for each requirement</li>
    <li>Gap classification: uncollected vs. collected-and-empty</li>
    <li>ATHENA surface: gap indicator on PARAM claims in uncollected domains</li>
    <li>Collection recommendation surface for requirements officers</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#5A2D82;margin-bottom:5px">ATHENA integration</div>
   <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin:0">When an analyst asks about a topic with no collection coverage, ARGUS-LACUNA surfaces a gap indicator alongside the PARAM badge: "No collection coverage exists for this domain — the absence of claims reflects a collection gap, not absence of the phenomenon." GC-2 policy decision required before deployment (gap indicator disclosure acceptability).</p>
  </div>
 </div>
 <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);border-top:1px solid var(--border);padding-top:8px"><strong>Naming:</strong> Argus-Panoptes was the all-seeing giant. LACUNA means gap or void. ARGUS-LACUNA is the all-seeing that reveals its own blind spots — the holes in an otherwise comprehensive surveillance picture.</div>
</div>

<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#5A2D82">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F0EBF9;color:#5A2D82">Year 4 · Q1 · Proposed</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F9EAEA;color:#8A2E2E">themis-warning</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#5A2D82">WSF / LACHESIS <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Weak Signal Fusion Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#5A2D82">The Measurer — finding meaning in what individually means nothing</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:10px">Monitors signals across active requirements and surfaces clusters that exceed a significance threshold their individual components do not reach. Classic intelligence failure pattern: individual signals, none actionable alone, crossing requirement boundaries or falling below significance thresholds — dots nobody connected because the connecting was nobody's job. LACHESIS makes cross-signal pattern recognition systematic.</div>
 <div class="card" style="background:#FBF0F0;border:1px solid #E8B4B4;padding:10px 12px;margin-bottom:10px;font-size:11.5px;color:#6B1818">
  <strong>Access control constraint:</strong> This service necessarily operates across requirement compartments. Every signal crossing a compartment boundary must be gated by PCES/AEGIS before surfacing. The service must be supervisory-facing only in initial deployment — not analyst-facing — until compartment crossing policy is formally established by General Counsel and the IOB.
 </div>
 <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#5A2D82;margin-bottom:5px">Data floor requirements</div>
 <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:8px">LACHESIS requires a minimum active requirement pool before cross-signal analysis produces statistically meaningful clusters. Estimated minimum: 30 simultaneous active requirements with populated signal data. Below this threshold the service produces noise, not signal.</p>
 <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);border-top:1px solid var(--border);padding-top:8px"><strong>Naming:</strong> LACHESIS is one of the three Fates — the one who measures the thread of life. The service measures signal threads across requirements to determine which, in combination, have reached significance.</div>
</div>

<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#5A2D82">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F0EBF9;color:#5A2D82">Year 4 · Q1 · Proposed</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F9EAEA;color:#8A2E2E">themis-warning</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#5A2D82">CRF / JANUS <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Cross-Requirement Fusion Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#5A2D82">The Two-Faced — seeing connections between what is being tracked separately</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:10px">Different analysts working different requirements may hold pieces of the same analytical picture. JANUS surfaces semantic correlations across active requirements where analysts on both sides have the appropriate access. The service does not reveal content across compartment boundaries — it surfaces the existence of a correlation and routes it to an analyst or supervisor who holds access to both sides.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:10px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#5A2D82;margin-bottom:5px">Correlation types</div>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.7;margin:0">
    <li>Entity overlap — same target entity appearing in multiple requirements under different vocabularies (OGS/YGGDRASIL enables this)</li>
    <li>Timeline correlation — events in separate requirements with temporally significant relationship</li>
    <li>Source overlap — same collector or reporting chain informing separate requirements</li>
    <li>Analytical conclusion convergence — independent requirements reaching similar assessments</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;margin-bottom:5px">Hard design constraints</div>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.7;margin:0">
    <li>PCES/AEGIS is authoritative — no correlation surfaces if either requirement's content would breach compartment policy</li>
    <li>Supervisory-only surface in all phases</li>
    <li>GC policy design required before any deployment</li>
    <li>Depends on OGS/YGGDRASIL for entity-aware matching</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
 </div>
 <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);border-top:1px solid var(--border);padding-top:8px"><strong>Naming:</strong> JANUS is the Roman god of beginnings, passages, and duality — depicted with two faces seeing in both directions simultaneously. The service sees both sides of a compartment boundary to surface connections neither side alone can see.</div>
</div>

</div>

<!-- SWS/SENTINEL spans full width -->
<div class="svc-card" style="border-left-color:#5A2D82;margin-bottom:20px">
 <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F0EBF9;color:#5A2D82">Year 4 · Q2 · Proposed — Most Architecturally Ambitious</span> <span class="phase-pill" style="background:#F9EAEA;color:#8A2E2E">themis-warning</span>
 <div class="svc-code" style="color:#5A2D82">SWS / SENTINEL <span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:500;opacity:.7">— Strategic Warning Service</span></div>
 <div class="svc-epithet" style="color:#5A2D82">The Strategic Watchkeeper — indicators of things that would fundamentally change the analytical picture</div>
 <div class="svc-fn" style="margin-bottom:12px">Specifically watches for indicators of strategic surprise — things that would fundamentally change the analytical picture if true, including things that have no direct historical precedent. Distinct from PYTHIA, which surfaces intelligence based on known patterns from similar requirements. SENTINEL is looking for the signatures of things that haven't happened in this pattern before — the early indicators of a genuinely novel development.</div>
 <div class="grid grid-3" style="gap:12px;margin-bottom:12px">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#5A2D82;margin-bottom:5px">Mechanism</div>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.7;margin:0">
    <li>Maintains a strategic indicator registry — conditions that would constitute strategic surprise for active analytical domains</li>
    <li>Monitors CASSANDRA anomaly outputs, LACHESIS cluster signals, ORACLE deviation events, and MNEMOSYNE pattern corpus</li>
    <li>Generates strategic warning indicators when combinations of signals cross threshold</li>
    <li>Routes to senior analysts and supervisors with full provenance of the contributing signals</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#5A2D82;margin-bottom:5px">Dependencies</div>
   <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.7;margin:0">
    <li>CASSANDRA — anomaly signals as precursor indicators</li>
    <li>WSF/LACHESIS — cross-requirement weak signal clusters</li>
    <li>ORACLE — outcome deviation from historical patterns</li>
    <li>MNEMOSYNE — institutional knowledge of prior near-misses</li>
    <li>OGS/YGGDRASIL — semantic grounding for entity-level indicator tracking</li>
   </ul>
  </div>
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10.5px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;margin-bottom:5px">Open research question</div>
   <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin:0">Can you reliably specify the signatures of strategic surprise without knowing what you will be surprised by? The strategic indicator registry requires domain experts to define what surprise would look like — which partially defeats the purpose. This is the hardest design problem in the intelligence layer. SENTINEL is architecturally last for this reason, not just because of data floor dependencies.</p>
  </div>
 </div>
 <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);border-top:1px solid var(--border);padding-top:8px"><strong>Naming:</strong> SENTINEL is the watchkeeper who sees what others miss because it is their specific job to watch. The strategic warning function has historically failed not because the signals weren't present but because nobody's job was to watch for the combination.</div>
</div>



<div style="background:#EEF2F8;border-radius:8px;padding:16px 20px;border:1px solid #C0CEDF">
 <h4 style="font-size:13px;margin-bottom:6px;color:#1B3A5C">The Compounding Advantage</h4>
 <p style="font-size:12.5px;line-height:1.65;color:var(--sub)">At Month 36, the agency has a platform that gets smarter with every matter it handles. Peer agencies using generic AI tools start from zero on each collection requirement. The agency using THEMIS and the intelligence layer starts from the accumulated knowledge of every matter it has ever handled — and the system knows how to apply that knowledge to the matter at hand. That gap widens every year. It cannot be closed by any external vendor because it is built on the agency's own data, the agency's own corrections, and the agency's own institutional knowledge.</p>
</div>
</div>

<!-- ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     SECTION 4 — OPERATING MODEL
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->
<div class="section" id="opmodel">
<div class="section-title">Operating Model</div>
<div class="section-sub">Three parallel tracks · Cross-team coordination mechanisms · Decision rights · Cadence structure</div>

<h3 class="subsec">Three Tracks</h3>
<div class="track-row"><div class="track-label-col" style="background:#6D4FB5;color:#fff">Research Track</div><div class="track-content-col"><h4>8–12 week research cycles · Pre-registered methodology</h4><p>Research and Red Team only. Research question locked before any data collected. Null results are valid outputs. Reports reviewed by Ethics Advisory Board before reaching leadership. Research team never takes sprint tickets. Independence is structural, not aspirational.</p></div></div>
<div class="track-row"><div class="track-label-col" style="background:#2E5FAD;color:#fff">Delivery Track</div><div class="track-content-col"><h4>2-week synchronized sprints · Platform and Intelligence teams</h4><p>Same sprint start/end dates across both engineering teams. Three swim lanes: roadmap (phase gate sequenced), red team response (severity-classified, never deprioritized below one sprint for P1), technical health (15–20% minimum per sprint). Intelligence team also runs model development stream (4–6 week model iterations, deployed when quality thresholds met, not on sprint boundary).</p></div></div>
<div class="track-row"><div class="track-label-col" style="background:#8B2222;color:#fff">Evaluation Track</div><div class="track-content-col"><h4>Continuous · Monthly report to Cell Lead and CTO simultaneously</h4><p>Two Red Team Evaluators run independent programs: Model/Prompt/Agent (continuous adversarial battery, monthly systematic sweep, full evaluation on every MDS-detected version change) and Platform Adversarial (IAS taxonomy monthly testing, MAS classifier evaluation bimonthly per modality, CVS adversarial patterns monthly, cryptographic chain quarterly). Not filtered through engineering team leads.</p></div></div>

<h3 class="subsec">Cadence Structure</h3>
<div class="cadence-grid">
 <div class="cadence-col" style="background:#EEF2F8;border-color:#C0CCE0"><h4 style="color:#1B3A5C">Daily</h4><div class="cadence-item">Each team runs its own standup</div><div class="cadence-item">No cross-team daily ritual</div></div>
 <div class="cadence-col" style="background:#EEF3FF;border-color:#B0C4E8"><h4 style="color:#2E5FAD">Biweekly</h4><div class="cadence-item">Platform sprint review + planning</div><div class="cadence-item">Intelligence sprint review + planning</div><div class="cadence-item">Research midpoint check-in</div><div class="cadence-item">Engineering Lead + ML Lead bilateral (30 min, service contract dependencies only)</div></div>
 <div class="cadence-col" style="background:#FFF7E8;border-color:#E0C880"><h4 style="color:#9A6800">Monthly</h4><div class="cadence-item">Cell-wide sync: all team leads + Cell Lead</div><div class="cadence-item">Red Team monthly report → Cell Lead + CTO (simultaneous)</div><div class="cadence-item">Corpus Quality metrics report</div><div class="cadence-item">Ethics Advisory Board written summary</div></div>
 <div class="cadence-col" style="background:#F2EEFD;border-color:#C8B8F0"><h4 style="color:#4A2FA8">Quarterly</h4><div class="cadence-item">Ethics Advisory Board review (data practices, methodology)</div><div class="cadence-item">Intelligence Oversight Board: red team findings, corpus quality, PGS policy changes</div><div class="cadence-item">Research cycle completion: findings brief</div><div class="cadence-item">CTO operating model review</div></div>
 <div class="cadence-col" style="background:#E6F5F3;border-color:#A0D0C8"><h4 style="color:#0D6B5E">Annually</h4><div class="cadence-item">Full charter review</div><div class="cadence-item">Academic partner joint review</div><div class="cadence-item">Intervention catalog standards review</div><div class="cadence-item">Cryptographic chain full audit + certificate</div></div>
</div>

<h3 class="subsec">Decision Rights</h3>
<table class="decision-table">
 <tr><th>Authority level</th><th>Owner</th><th>Examples</th></tr>
 <tr><td>Each team independently</td><td>Team Lead</td><td>Research agenda and methodology (R+RT) · THEMIS service architecture and APIs (Platform) · Calibration model design and training (Intelligence)</td></tr>
 <tr><td>Cross-team agreement required</td><td>Engineering Lead + Intelligence ML Lead</td><td>TCS/MIMIR service contract changes · New THEMIS service proposals · Changes to ATHENA intervention design affecting calibration architecture · FGTS corpus quality threshold changes</td></tr>
 <tr><td>Cell Lead decision</td><td>Cell Lead</td><td>Capacity reallocation between teams · New external partnerships · Platform-wide standards changes · Emergency response to P0 red team findings</td></tr>
 <tr><td>Intelligence Oversight Board</td><td>Governance Committee</td><td>PGS policy rule changes · New interaction class definitions · Red team findings with firm-wide liability implications · FGTS corpus rollback decisions · MDS version pin release</td></tr>
</table>

<h3 class="subsec">Cross-Team Coordination Mechanisms</h3>
<div class="grid grid-2">
 <div class="card" style="border-left:4px solid #6D4FB5"><h4 style="color:#6D4FB5">Research → Engineering Translation</h4><p>Research cycle completes → HF Researchers produce design brief (behavioral/UX requirements, not specs) → Interface+Integration Engineer (Platform) + Intelligence ML Lead jointly review → Convert to backlog items. Typical lag: 2–4 weeks. Prevents research findings reaching engineering as vague mandates.</p></div>
 <div class="card" style="border-left:4px solid #8B2222"><h4 style="color:#8B2222">Red Team Response Protocol</h4><p><strong>P0 Critical:</strong> Cell Lead within 24h, emergency sprint work authorized. <strong>P1 High:</strong> Next sprint backlog, Cell Lead notified. <strong>P2 Medium:</strong> Normal backlog prioritization, red team follow-up after fix. <strong>P3 Low:</strong> Taxonomy maintenance, informs future research cycles.</p></div>
 <div class="card" style="border-left:4px solid #2E5FAD"><h4 style="color:#2E5FAD">Platform ↔ Intelligence Service Contract</h4><p>TCS/MIMIR boundary: Platform owns the service infrastructure, Intelligence owns the calibration models it runs. Changes to the service contract require joint sign-off from Engineering Lead and Intelligence ML Lead. Neither team can make unilateral contract changes. Changes require a migration plan both teams agree to before the sprint begins.</p></div>
 <div class="card" style="border-left:4px solid #0D6B5E"><h4 style="color:#0D6B5E">FGTS Quality Feedback Loop</h4><p>Corpus Quality Specialist (Research team) ↔ Calibration Engineer (Intelligence team). Weekly standing touchpoint. Corpus Quality Specialist surfaces review queue patterns; Calibration Engineer responds with model adjustments. Runs outside sprint planning — operational rhythm not a project.</p></div>
</div>
</div>

<h3 class="subsec">Source Type Badges — Design Rationale</h3>

<div class="card" style="border-left:4px solid #1A9E8C;margin-bottom:20px;padding:16px 20px">
 <h4 style="color:#0D6B5E;margin-bottom:8px">Why source type is surfaced at claim level</h4>
 <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.7;margin-bottom:10px">LLMs produce uniformly fluent output regardless of how a claim was generated — whether retrieved from a specific document, inferred from training weights, synthesized across partial sources, or derived from an AI-generated transcript of a source debriefing. That uniformity is the problem. Fluency acts as a proxy for credibility in standard interfaces, and the source type of a claim has direct implications for what verification is even possible.</p>
 <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.7">A claim marked GRND can be checked against a specific intelligence report. A claim marked PARAM cannot — the appropriate response is independent collection or explicit acknowledgment that the claim is unverifiable through normal analyst workflow. A claim marked TRANSCRIPT requires checking against the recording itself, not the report it was transcribed from — because the transcript is a derivative artifact with its own error rate. These are qualitatively different epistemic tasks in the IC tradecraft sense, and the interface must make the distinction visible before the analyst acts.</p>
</div>

<div class="grid grid-2" style="margin-bottom:20px;gap:16px">
 <div class="card" style="border-top:4px solid #1B3A5C">
  <h4 style="color:#1B3A5C;margin-bottom:8px">Text-derived badges (3)</h4>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin-bottom:8px">Claims derived from text sources — retrieved documents, training weights, or synthesized from multiple partial matches. These are the original three source types from the initial design. TCS/MIMIR calibration models have the richest verification outcome history for these types.</p>
  <div style="display:flex;gap:8px;flex-wrap:wrap">
   <span class="src-badge" style="color:#2E5FAD;border-color:#2E5FAD;font-size:11px;padding:3px 8px">GRND</span>
   <span class="src-badge" style="color:#9A6800;border-color:#9A6800;font-size:11px;padding:3px 8px">PARAM</span>
   <span class="src-badge" style="color:#6B7590;border-color:#6B7590;font-size:11px;padding:3px 8px">SYNTH</span>
  </div>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:4px solid #8B2222">
  <h4 style="color:#8B2222;margin-bottom:8px">Media-derived badges (5)</h4>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin-bottom:8px">Claims derived from AI analysis of media artifacts or from OCR extraction. These require separate TCS/MIMIR calibration streams because the cognitive task, failure mode taxonomy, and available verification actions differ materially from text-derived claims. MAS/EIDOLON authenticity risk scores are integrated for VIDEO, AUDIO, IMAGE, and TRANSCRIPT badges.</p>
  <div style="display:flex;gap:8px;flex-wrap:wrap">
   <span class="src-badge" style="color:#0D6B5E;border-color:#0D6B5E;font-size:11px;padding:3px 8px">TRANSCRIPT</span>
   <span class="src-badge" style="color:#4A2FA8;border-color:#4A2FA8;font-size:11px;padding:3px 8px">VIDEO</span>
   <span class="src-badge" style="color:#6D4FB5;border-color:#6D4FB5;font-size:11px;padding:3px 8px">AUDIO</span>
   <span class="src-badge" style="color:#8B2222;border-color:#8B2222;font-size:11px;padding:3px 8px">IMAGE</span>
   <span class="src-badge" style="color:#7A5000;border-color:#7A5000;font-size:11px;padding:3px 8px">OCR</span>
  </div>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- Key design decisions -->
<div class="grid grid-2" style="margin-bottom:24px;gap:12px">
 <div style="background:#E6F5F3;border:1px solid #A0D0C4;border-radius:8px;padding:14px 16px">
  <h4 style="color:#0D6B5E;font-size:12px;margin-bottom:6px">TRANSCRIPT is not GRND</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:#0A4A40;line-height:1.6">An AI-generated transcript of a video source debriefing is not equivalent to a retrieved source document. The transcript has its own error rate — ASR (automatic speech recognition) accuracy varies by audio quality, speaker accent, technical terminology, and background noise. A claim sourced from a transcript inherits the transcript's accuracy profile, not the authority of the original recording. Showing TRANSCRIPT rather than GRND makes this derivation visible at the claim level. The verification action set reflects this: "Transcript may be inaccurate" routes to MAS/EIDOLON for transcript accuracy corpus update — a different signal from "Misrepresents source," which routes as a synthesis failure.</p>
 </div>
 <div style="background:#FFF7E8;border:1px solid #E0C880;border-radius:8px;padding:14px 16px">
  <h4 style="color:#9A6800;font-size:12px;margin-bottom:6px">Confidence ceiling concept</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:#7A5000;line-height:1.6">Source type creates an upper bound on TCS/MIMIR confidence scores independent of the model's self-reported confidence. An OCR-derived claim from a region with 62% OCR confidence cannot carry a HIGH confidence badge regardless of how confidently the model stated it — the accuracy of the extracted text is the binding constraint. Similarly, a claim derived from a media artifact with a high MAS/EIDOLON authenticity risk score receives a reduced confidence ceiling: the claim may accurately represent what the artifact showed, but what the artifact showed may itself be fabricated. The badge system surfaces both the TCS confidence score and the source type — readers see both signals independently.</p>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     SECTION 6 — SEQUENCE DIAGRAM
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->
<div class="section" id="sequence">
<div class="section-title">Analyst Session Flow</div>
<div class="section-sub">C4 Dynamic Diagram — 9-phase sequence from session setup through dissemination with full provenance record.</div>
<div class="mermaid-wrap">
<pre class="mermaid">
sequenceDiagram
    autonumber
    actor A as Analyst
    participant AT as ATHENA
    participant PCES as PCES/AEGIS
    participant PGS as PGS/NOMOS
    participant IAS as IAS/SCUDO
    participant MIMIR as MIMIR Retrieval
    participant IG as Inference Gateway
    participant CVS as CVS/VERITAS
    participant ERAS as ERAS/LOGOS
    participant TCS as TCS/MIMIR
    participant FGTS as FGTS/ALETHEIA
    participant KB as Kafka+MOIRAI

    Note over A,KB: PHASE 1 - SESSION SETUP
    A->>AT: Declare session intent - task, decision, consequence
    AT->>TCS: Fetch analyst calibration profile and cold start state
    TCS-->>AT: Prior RAI scores and domain calibration state
    A->>AT: Complete Intention Gate - prior belief and falsification condition

    Note over A,KB: PHASE 2 - SAFETY GATES
    A->>AT: Submit query
    AT->>PCES: Request + session context + requirement scope
    PCES->>PCES: Compartment check, CoI detection, requirement scope validation
    alt Compartment violation
        PCES-->>AT: Block
    else Cleared
        PCES->>PGS: Cleared interaction
    end
    PGS->>PGS: Interaction classification, PII detection, policy evaluation
    alt Policy block
        PGS-->>AT: Block
    else Approved
        PGS->>MIMIR: Retrieval query
        PGS->>IG: Policy-approved signal
    end

    Note over A,KB: PHASE 3 - RETRIEVAL AND SCREENING
    MIMIR->>IAS: Retrieved chunks - adversarial pre-screen
    IAS->>IAS: Taxonomy matching + embedding similarity + heuristic scoring
    alt Chunk quarantined above 0.85 threshold
        IAS->>KB: InjectionScreeningEvent
    end
    IAS-->>MIMIR: Sanitised chunks with risk scores
    MIMIR-->>IG: Screened chunks + match scores + provenance trace

    Note over A,KB: PHASE 4 - INFERENCE
    IG->>IG: Inject system prompt, parameters, context
    IG->>IG: Capture full context snapshot
    IG-->>AT: Context snapshot for Inspector
    IG->>IG: Model inference call to vendor API

    Note over A,KB: PHASE 5 - GENERATION-TIME SERVICES
    par CVS - synchronous, blocks render on No Corroboration
        IG->>CVS: Response text - extract citations
        CVS->>CVS: Resolve against IC intelligence databases
        alt No Corroboration
            CVS-->>AT: Hard interrupt before response renders
        else Verified or Unresolved
            CVS-->>IG: Citation manifest
        end
        CVS->>KB: Signed CitationVerificationEvent
    and ERAS - async reasoning capture
        IG->>ERAS: Turn content
        ERAS->>ERAS: Claim indexing and unsupported claim detection
        ERAS->>KB: Signed ReasoningRecord
    end

    Note over A,KB: PHASE 6 - CONFIDENCE AND DISPLAY
    AT->>TCS: Claim list with type, domain, model version, source type
    TCS-->>AT: Calibrated scores - HIGH / MED / UNVERIFIED per claim
    AT-->>A: Response + confidence badges + source type badges + verification queue

    Note over A,KB: PHASE 7 - PROVENANCE WRITES
    par All services sign and publish
        PCES->>KB: Signed CompartmentAnnotationEvent + prev_event_hash
        PGS->>KB: Signed PolicyEvaluationEvent
        IG->>KB: Signed ContextSnapshotEvent
    end
    Note over KB: MOIRAI validates signatures, updates hash chain, writes to Neo4j. Every 24h digest to RFC 3161 TSA.

    Note over A,KB: PHASE 8 - VERIFICATION AND CALIBRATION
    A->>AT: Expand provenance panel - review source
    AT->>KB: Fetch source provenance and ERAS reasoning
    KB-->>AT: Source passage + provenance chain + reasoning record
    A->>AT: Verification action - typed by source type
    AT->>FGTS: Correction event with action type and consequence weight
    FGTS->>FGTS: Compute 5-factor confidence weight
    alt Weight below minimum threshold
        FGTS->>FGTS: Route to human review queue
    else Above threshold
        FGTS->>TCS: Weighted correction signal
        TCS->>TCS: Update domain calibration model
    end
    FGTS->>KB: Signed CorrectionEvent
    TCS->>KB: Signed CalibrationUpdateEvent

    Note over A,KB: PHASE 9 - EXPORT WITH PROVENANCE GATE
    A->>AT: Request export
    alt Unverified HIGH confidence claims remain
        AT-->>A: Export blocked - supervisor acknowledgment required
    else Queue clear
        AT->>KB: Assemble canonical provenance record
        KB-->>AT: Full provenance - model version, params, claims, verification states, ERAS record, TCS RAI snapshot
        AT-->>A: Export artifact with complete signed provenance record
    end
</pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class="section-title">Intervention Catalog</div>

<script>
var _mermaidLoaded = false;
var _mermaidLoading = false;
var _mermaidQueue = [];

function loadMermaid(callback) {
  if (_mermaidLoaded) { callback(); return; }
  _mermaidQueue.push(callback);
  if (_mermaidLoading) return;
  _mermaidLoading = true;
  var s = document.createElement('script');
  s.src = 'https://cdnjs.cloudflare.com/ajax/libs/mermaid/10.6.1/mermaid.min.js';
  s.onload = function() {
    try {
      mermaid.initialize({
        startOnLoad: false,
        theme: 'default',
        sequence: {diagramMarginX:20,diagramMarginY:10,actorMargin:40,width:150,height:55,boxMargin:8,messageMargin:25}
      });
    } catch(e) { console.warn('mermaid init:', e); }
    _mermaidLoaded = true;
    _mermaidLoading = false;
    _mermaidQueue.forEach(function(fn){ try { fn(); } catch(e){ console.warn('mermaid queue cb:', e); } });
    _mermaidQueue = [];
  };
  s.onerror = function() {
    console.warn('mermaid failed to load');
    _mermaidLoading = false;
    _mermaidQueue = [];
  };
  document.head.appendChild(s);
}

function show(id,btn){
  document.querySelectorAll('.section').forEach(function(s){ s.classList.remove('active'); });
  document.querySelectorAll('.tab-btn').forEach(function(b){ b.classList.remove('active'); });
  document.getElementById(id).classList.add('active');
  btn.classList.add('active');
  if (id === 'sequence' || id === 'techref') {
    loadMermaid(function() {
      try {
        var el = document.getElementById(id).querySelector('.mermaid');
        if (el && !el.dataset.processed) {
          mermaid.run({ nodes: [el] });
          el.dataset.processed = 'true';
        }
      } catch(e) { console.warn('mermaid render:', e); }
    });
  }
}
</script>

<div style="display:flex;gap:16px;margin-bottom:10px;flex-wrap:wrap">
 <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:6px"><span style="width:28px;height:20px;background:#1B3A5C;border-radius:3px;display:inline-block"></span><span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:700;color:#1B3A5C">B — Both</span></div>
 <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:6px"><span style="width:28px;height:20px;background:#DBEAFE;border-radius:3px;display:inline-block;border:1px solid #93C5FD"></span><span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:700;color:#1E40AF">P — Producer</span><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">writes events or data to the service</span></div>
 <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:6px"><span style="width:28px;height:20px;background:#D1FAE5;border-radius:3px;display:inline-block;border:1px solid #6EE7B7"></span><span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:700;color:#065F46">C — Consumer</span><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">reads from the service</span></div>
 <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:6px"><span style="width:28px;height:20px;background:#F9FAFB;border-radius:3px;display:inline-block;border:1px solid #E5E7EB"></span><span style="font-size:12px;color:var(--muted)">— No direct integration</span></div>
</div>
<div style="display:flex;gap:12px;margin-bottom:14px;flex-wrap:wrap">
 <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:6px"><span style="width:18px;height:14px;background:#1B3A5C;border-radius:2px;display:inline-block"></span><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">Original 9 THEMIS services</span></div>
 <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:6px"><span style="width:18px;height:14px;background:#0D7060;border-radius:2px;display:inline-block"></span><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">Addendum A — 5 new platform services (CVS, MDS, IAS, MAS, DPS)</span></div>
 <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:6px"><span style="width:18px;height:14px;background:#5A3DBF;border-radius:2px;display:inline-block"></span><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">Intelligence Layer — 6 Year 2-3 services. ATHENA interventions have limited current integration; connections shown where documented in the intelligence layer proposal.</span></div>
</div>

<h3 class="subsec" id="tr-arch">C4 Container Architecture Diagram</h3>
<p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);margin-bottom:14px;line-height:1.6">The THEMIS and ATHENA platform at the container level. Shows all 22 platform services, Norse shared services, external systems, and primary data flows. P (Producer) relationships flow as signed Kafka events to Provenance/MOIRAI. C (Consumer) relationships are synchronous HTTP reads.</p>
<div class="mermaid-wrap">
<pre class="mermaid">

    title THEMIS and ATHENA - Platform Architecture (C4 Container)

    Person(analyst, "Analyst", "AI-assisted analysis, verification, annotation, export")

    Container_Boundary(b_athena, "ATHENA Interface") {
        Container(athena, "ATHENA", "React / Web", "28-intervention calibrated analysis interface. Verification queue, confidence badges, session intent, dissemination gate, multi-analyst framework.")
    }
