 <tr style="background:#EEEBFF"><td colspan="3" style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#4A2FA8;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;padding:5px 10px">Year 2 — Intelligence Layer</td></tr>
 <tr><td>Agent task memory (Redis + MOIRAI milestones)</td><td>STOA / PYTHIA services deployed</td><td><span class="badge" style="background:#4A2FA8;color:#fff">Year 2</span></td></tr>
 <tr><td>MEMORY·TSK badge + TCS calibration stream</td><td>Agent task memory operational</td><td><span class="badge" style="background:#4A2FA8;color:#fff">Year 2</span></td></tr>
</table>

<!-- Open design questions -->
<h3 class="subsec">Open Design Questions</h3>
<div style="display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:10px">
 <div class="ac-notice" style="background:#EDE8FF;border:1px solid #C4B5FD">
  <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#3B2A6B;margin-bottom:5px">Design Question 1 — Matter Knowledge Graduation Threshold · Before Phase 5-6</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:#2A1A5B;line-height:1.65;margin:0">The graduation threshold is not a technical question — it is a professional judgment about how much confirmation is sufficient before a claim is elevated to established institutional fact. Too low: unverified claims pollute the assessment knowledge corpus. Too high: nothing graduates and the layer provides no benefit. The threshold should be configurable by requirement class and requires Intelligence Oversight Board determination before Phase 5-6 implementation.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="ac-notice" style="background:#EDE8FF;border:1px solid #C4B5FD">
  <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#3B2A6B;margin-bottom:5px">Design Question 2 — Session Summary Schema · Co-designed in Phase 3-4</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:#2A1A5B;line-height:1.65;margin:0">The schema must be co-designed with the ATHENA interface team and with practicing analysts. It needs to be rich enough to be useful but structured enough to be precisely retrievable. The schema design work should begin in Phase 3-4 alongside MOIRAI — not after MOIRAI is built, because summary storage depends on MOIRAI node type design.</p>
 </div>
</div>
</div>

<!-- ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     COMPETENCE AXIS — ADDENDUM D
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->
<div class="section" id="competence">
<div class="section-title">Competence Axis</div>
<div class="section-sub">Addendum D — A proposed fourth accountability dimension. Partially buildable now. Hard enforcement gated on six open research questions.</div>

<style>
.comp-zone{display:inline-block;padding:3px 10px;border-radius:4px;font-size:10px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.06em;text-transform:uppercase}
.comp-schema{background:#F4F6FA;color:#1A2A3A;padding:12px 16px;border-radius:6px;border:1px solid var(--border);font-family:'SF Mono',Consolas,monospace;font-size:10.5px;line-height:1.65;overflow:auto;margin:8px 0 12px}
</style>

<!-- Governing concept -->
<div style="background:#6B3FA8;border-radius:10px;padding:20px 24px;color:#fff;margin-bottom:24px">
 <div style="display:grid;grid-template-columns:auto 1fr;gap:24px;align-items:start">
  <div>
   <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:rgba(255,255,255,.55);text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.12em;margin-bottom:4px">Proposed Axis IV</div>
   <div style="font-size:28px;font-weight:800;color:#fff;margin-bottom:2px">Competence</div>
   <div style="font-size:13px;color:rgba(255,255,255,.75)">Can this AI system reliably perform this task at all?</div>
  </div>
  <div>
   <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:rgba(255,255,255,.85);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:10px">The three existing axes ask how good the AI output is. The Competence axis asks a prior question: is reliable output achievable on this task by this system? An assessment can pass Origin, Currency, and Trust — provenance-perfect, current, well-calibrated — and still be unreliable if the task exceeds the model's actual capability.</p>
   <div style="display:flex;gap:8px;flex-wrap:wrap">
    <span style="background:rgba(255,255,255,.15);padding:3px 10px;border-radius:4px;font-size:11px;color:#fff">Not a subset of Trust</span>
    <span style="background:rgba(255,255,255,.15);padding:3px 10px;border-radius:4px;font-size:11px;color:#fff">Not a subset of Origin</span>
    <span style="background:rgba(255,255,255,.15);padding:3px 10px;border-radius:4px;font-size:11px;color:#fff">Not a subset of Currency</span>
    <span style="background:rgba(255,255,255,.15);padding:3px 10px;border-radius:4px;font-size:11px;color:#fff">Proposed — no implementation timeline</span>
   </div>
  </div>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- Why distinct -->
<div class="card" style="border-left:4px solid #6B3FA8;padding:12px 16px;margin-bottom:20px">
 <h4 style="color:#6B3FA8;font-size:12px;margin-bottom:6px">The Specific Failure Pattern</h4>
 <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin:0">Large language models produce outputs whether or not they are capable of producing reliable outputs. RLHF training systematically rewards confident-sounding responses and penalizes "I cannot reliably answer this." The result: fluent, confident, structurally coherent output on tasks the model has no reliable competence to perform — indistinguishable at the interface from output the model is genuinely capable of producing. The analyst has no signal.</p>
</div>

<!-- Six failure modes -->
<h3 class="subsec">Six Failure Modes</h3>
<div class="grid grid-3" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:24px">
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #6B3FA8;padding:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#6B3FA8;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:5px">1 — Knowledge Boundary</div>
  <h4 style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:5px">Violations</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">Events after training cutoff, classified programs never trained on, proprietary organizational knowledge. Neither retrieval nor training can provide grounding. The response is generated from statistical patterns of adjacent data — which may bear no reliable relationship to the actual answer.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #6B3FA8;padding:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#6B3FA8;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:5px">2 — Task Capability</div>
  <h4 style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:5px">Mismatch</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">Precise mathematical verification, cryptographic analysis, signals processing, network mapping from indirect indicators. The model produces output. It may appear correct. But it has no reliable mechanism to distinguish its correct outputs from its incorrect ones in unevaluated domains.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #8B2222;padding:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:5px">3 — Out of Distribution</div>
  <h4 style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:5px">Queries</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">Novel adversary patterns with no historical precedent. Geopolitical configurations without analogues. Domains where training data was sparse or deliberately withheld. The model has no internal signal it is OOD. It generates the same confident output as an in-distribution query.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #9A6800;padding:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#9A6800;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:5px">4 — Compound Capability</div>
  <h4 style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:5px">Degradation</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">Reliable on each component of a complex task; unreliable on their combination in ways not predictable from component performance. Accurately characterizes technical programs and separately performs geopolitical analysis — fails reliably on their integration into a timeline-linked assessment.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #0D6B5E;padding:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#0D6B5E;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:5px">5 — Epistemic</div>
  <h4 style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:5px">Non-Declaration</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">The model fails to declare that it does not know. RLHF penalizes refusal relative to apparent helpfulness. Models that appear to express uncertainty often do so performatively — hedging in the preamble, then producing a confident conclusion anyway.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #8B2222;padding:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:5px">6 — Adversarial</div>
  <h4 style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:5px">Competence Probing</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">Deliberate D&D technique: query the AI in known capability gap areas to generate confident confabulations, then introduce those AI-sourced confabulations into the analytical record. Distinct from corpus poisoning (retrieval layer) and prompt injection (instruction layer). Attacks the model's tendency to generate confident output in gap areas.</p>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- Proposed services -->
<h3 class="subsec">Proposed Services</h3>
<div class="grid grid-2" style="gap:16px;margin-bottom:20px">

 <!-- CPS/APORIA -->
 <div class="card" style="border-top:4px solid #6B3FA8">
  <div style="display:flex;justify-content:space-between;align-items:flex-start;margin-bottom:8px">
   <div>
    <div class="svc-code" style="color:#6B3FA8">CPS / APORIA</div>
    <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">Capability Profiling Service · themis-quality</div>
   </div>
   <span class="badge" style="background:#EDE8FF;color:#4A2FA8;white-space:nowrap">New · Forward</span>
  </div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin-bottom:10px">Maintains a version-controlled capability taxonomy for each deployed model, classifying analytical task types into three zones. Every CapabilityWarningEvent signed and appended to the MOIRAI provenance chain.</p>
  <div style="display:flex;gap:6px;margin-bottom:10px">
   <span class="comp-zone" style="background:#E6F5F3;color:#0D6B5E">Green — Validated</span>
   <span class="comp-zone" style="background:#FFF7E8;color:#9A6800">Amber — Partial</span>
   <span class="comp-zone" style="background:#FBF0F0;color:#8B2222">Red — Beyond Capability</span>
  </div>
  <div class="comp-schema">
<span style="color:#1E40AF">interface</span> CapabilityTaxonomyEntry {<br>
&nbsp;&nbsp;task_type: <span style="color:#0D6B5E">string</span>;<br>
&nbsp;&nbsp;zone: <span style="color:#9A6800">'green'</span> | <span style="color:#9A6800">'amber'</span> | <span style="color:#9A6800">'red'</span>;<br>
&nbsp;&nbsp;confidence: <span style="color:#0D6B5E">number</span>; <span style="color:#5A6480">// 0–1</span><br>
&nbsp;&nbsp;red_team_validated: <span style="color:#0D6B5E">boolean</span>;<br>
&nbsp;&nbsp;version: <span style="color:#0D6B5E">string</span>;<br>
}
  </div>
  <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);border-top:1px solid var(--border);padding-top:8px"><strong>Gate:</strong> No deployment until taxonomy has ≥60 days red team evaluation data. Red zone triggers hard TCS confidence ceiling — HIGH and MEDIUM blocked regardless of calibration score.</div>
 </div>

 <!-- ODS/LETHE -->
 <div class="card" style="border-top:4px solid #1B3A5C">
  <div style="display:flex;justify-content:space-between;align-items:flex-start;margin-bottom:8px">
   <div>
    <div class="svc-code" style="color:#1B3A5C">ODS / LETHE</div>
    <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">Out-of-Distribution Screening Service · themis-quality</div>
   </div>
   <span class="badge" style="background:#EEF2F8;color:#1B3A5C;white-space:nowrap">New · Advisory only</span>
  </div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin-bottom:10px">Applies lightweight OOD screening at query ingestion before retrieval and inference. Embedding distance from training-distribution query cluster centroids, configurable threshold by interaction class.</p>
  <div style="background:#FBF0F0;border:1px solid #E8B4B4;border-radius:5px;padding:8px 10px;margin-bottom:10px;font-size:11px;color:#6B1818">
   <strong>Design constraint:</strong> OOD detection for autoregressive generative models is an open research problem. High expected false positive rates. Must be deployed advisory-only. Do not apply hard constraints until false positive rate is characterized on ≥500 operational queries.
  </div>
  <div class="comp-schema">
ODS.screen(query) → {<br>
&nbsp;&nbsp;distance: <span style="color:#0D6B5E">number</span>,<br>
&nbsp;&nbsp;threshold: <span style="color:#0D6B5E">number</span>,<br>
&nbsp;&nbsp;flagged: <span style="color:#0D6B5E">boolean</span>,<br>
&nbsp;&nbsp;confidence: <span style="color:#9A6800">'advisory'</span> <span style="color:#5A6480">// always</span><br>
}
  </div>
  <div style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);border-top:1px solid var(--border);padding-top:8px"><strong>Naming:</strong> LETHE — the Greek river of forgetfulness, what lies beyond the boundary of what can be known or recalled. ODS/LETHE surfaces queries at the boundary of what the model can know.</div>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- Service enhancements -->
<h3 class="subsec">Existing Service Enhancements</h3>
<div class="grid grid-3" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:20px">
 <div class="card" style="padding:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:5px">IAS / SCUDO</div>
  <h4 style="font-size:12px;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Capability Probe Detection Layer</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55">New query-layer classifier alongside existing adversarial injection classifiers. Detects query patterns characteristic of adversarial competence probing. Initial approach: signature matching + embedding similarity against red-team probe corpus. ML classifier is Year 2 (requires labeled training data). Flagged queries: advisory signal, not blocked. Logged to MOIRAI, included in IOB monthly report.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="padding:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#0D6B5E;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:5px">ERAS / LOGOS</div>
  <h4 style="font-size:12px;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Capability-Limit Reasoning Patterns</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55;margin-bottom:6px">New detection module for reasoning patterns characteristic of capability-limit operation:</p>
  <ul style="font-size:11px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.65">
   <li><strong>Circular reasoning</strong> — conclusion restates premises</li>
   <li><strong>Hedge + confident conclusion</strong> — uncertainty preamble, confident assertion</li>
   <li><strong>Self-contradiction</strong> — asserts X, contradicts X later</li>
   <li><strong>Specificity inversion</strong> — more confident in conclusion than in evidence</li>
  </ul>
  <p style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);margin-top:6px">Advisory signals only. Inline icon in ATHENA response view, expands on click.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="padding:12px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#1A9E8C;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:5px">TCS / MIMIR</div>
  <h4 style="font-size:12px;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Capability-Stratified Calibration</h4>
  <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55;margin-bottom:6px">Adds capability zone as a fourth stratification variable alongside (analyst, claim type, domain, model version).</p>
  <ul style="font-size:11px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.65">
   <li>Amber-zone calibration maintained separately from green-zone on same domain — pooling the data produces misleading signals</li>
   <li>Red-zone: hard TCS confidence ceiling. HIGH and MEDIUM confidence blocked regardless of calibration score</li>
   <li>An analyst's green-zone accuracy does not inform amber-zone calibration</li>
  </ul>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- ATHENA surface -->
<h3 class="subsec">ATHENA Competence Axis Surface</h3>
<p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin-bottom:12px">Three new interface elements. All advisory in Phase 1 — no hard blocks until false positive rates are characterized.</p>
<table class="decision-table" style="margin-bottom:20px">
 <tr><th>Element</th><th>Trigger</th><th>Presentation</th></tr>
 <tr><td><strong>Competence Zone Indicator</strong></td><td>Session includes amber or red zone query (CPS/APORIA)</td><td>Amber: "This query type is in a partially validated capability area. Verify outputs independently." Red: "This query type is outside this system's validated capability. A supervisor has been notified."</td></tr>
 <tr><td><strong>OOD Distance Signal</strong></td><td>Query embedding distance exceeds threshold (ODS/LETHE)</td><td>Optional panel, collapsed by default. Designed for supervisors and power users. Shows distance score and threshold. Not shown to general analyst population.</td></tr>
 <tr><td><strong>Capability-Limit Reasoning Flag</strong></td><td>ERAS detects capability-limit reasoning pattern</td><td>Subtle inline icon adjacent to the claim. Expands on click to show detected patterns and what they indicate about this response's reliability.</td></tr>
</table>

<!-- Implementation posture -->
<h3 class="subsec">Implementation Posture</h3>
<table class="decision-table" style="margin-bottom:20px">
 <tr><th>Phase</th><th>Capabilities</th><th>Gate to next phase</th></tr>
 <tr><td><span class="badge" style="background:#E6F5F3;color:#0D6B5E">Phase 1<br>Advisory</span></td><td>CPS/APORIA with red-team taxonomy (advisory signals only). ODS/LETHE monitoring mode — no analyst surface. ERAS competence-limit detection for supervisors opt-in.</td><td>Minimum 6 months operational data. False positive rates characterized. No hard blocks.</td></tr>
 <tr><td><span class="badge" style="background:#FFF7E8;color:#9A6800">Phase 2<br>Soft</span></td><td>CPS/APORIA red zone triggers supervisory notification. ODS/LETHE amber flags to analysts (collapsed). IAS/SCUDO probe detection advisory. TCS capability-stratified calibration active.</td><td>False positive rate below 15% on amber zone classification. Red team confirms probe detection taxonomy adequacy.</td></tr>
 <tr><td><span class="badge" style="background:#FBF0F0;color:#8B2222">Phase 3<br>Hard</span></td><td>Red zone queries produce hard dissemination constraints equivalent to Deadline-Critical hard constraints in the Trust axis.</td><td style="color:#8B2222;font-weight:600">Research questions 1 and 2 must have tractable solutions validated on intelligence-domain queries. This gate is on research maturity, not calendar.</td></tr>
</table>

<!-- Six open research questions -->
<h3 class="subsec">Six Open Research Questions</h3>
<p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin-bottom:14px">These must be substantially answered before the Competence axis can be implemented as hard enforcement. They represent the current boundary of the field.</p>
<div style="display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:10px">
 <div class="ac-notice" style="background:#F5F0FF;border:1px solid #C4A8F0">
  <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#6B3FA8;margin-bottom:4px">RQ1 — Reliable Epistemic Self-Assessment in LLMs</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:#3A1A70;line-height:1.6;margin:0">Models do not reliably know what they do not know. RLHF penalizes refusal — models appear to hedge but resolve to confident conclusions anyway. Constitutional approaches, capability-aware fine-tuning, and explicit capability declaration training objectives are active research directions. No production-ready solution exists.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="ac-notice" style="background:#F5F0FF;border:1px solid #C4A8F0">
  <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#6B3FA8;margin-bottom:4px">RQ2 — OOD Detection for Autoregressive Generative Models</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:#3A1A70;line-height:1.6;margin:0">For autoregressive generative models, every output is "in distribution" from the model's perspective — it always generates the most probable next tokens. There is no reliable internal signal that the query is outside training distribution. Embedding-distance heuristics have partial validity but high false positive rates. MoE routing confidence, retrieval-augmented uncertainty estimation, and ensemble disagreement are under research — none production-ready.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="ac-notice" style="background:#F5F0FF;border:1px solid #C4A8F0">
  <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#6B3FA8;margin-bottom:4px">RQ3 — Ground Truth for Capability Boundary Assessment in Intelligence Domains</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:#3A1A70;line-height:1.6;margin:0">Ground truth in intelligence domains is often classified, delayed, or impossible to produce without real-world events providing the answer. The capability taxonomy is only as reliable as the red team's ability to generate representative test cases — a process that is labor-intensive and always behind the frontier of novel query types.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="ac-notice" style="background:#F5F0FF;border:1px solid #C4A8F0">
  <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#6B3FA8;margin-bottom:4px">RQ4 — Compound Capability Interaction</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:#3A1A70;line-height:1.6;margin:0">Capability assessment for individual task types does not reliably predict performance on their combination. Compositional generalization research provides theoretical grounding but has not been applied to intelligence-domain analytical tasks at operational complexity. Systematic compositional capability evaluation is a large-scale effort, not a near-term research answer.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="ac-notice" style="background:#F5F0FF;border:1px solid #C4A8F0">
  <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#6B3FA8;margin-bottom:4px">RQ5 — Capability Boundary Shifts Under Adversarial Information Conditions</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:#3A1A70;line-height:1.6;margin:0">Capability boundaries that hold under benign conditions may shift when the model operates on adversarially selected information. This problem sits at the intersection of adversarial ML, capability evaluation, and intelligence tradecraft — a combination that has not been studied systematically. No established research program exists for this specific threat model.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="ac-notice" style="background:#F5F0FF;border:1px solid #C4A8F0">
  <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#6B3FA8;margin-bottom:4px">RQ6 — Training Objective Conflict</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:#3A1A70;line-height:1.6;margin:0">RLHF rewards helpfulness and penalizes refusal. Any Competence axis surface that relies on the model's own uncertainty expression works against the training objective that shaped the model's behavior. This is a training-time problem. Deployment-layer approaches are a holding action. Research direction: specify capability declaration as a requirement for future model procurement or fine-tuning partnerships.</p>
 </div>
</div>
</div>


<!-- ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     TRAINING DESIGN
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->
<div class="section" id="training">
<div class="section-title">ATHENA User Training Design</div>
<div class="section-sub">Governing principles · Curriculum architecture · Delivery framework · v1.0</div>

<!-- Problem statement -->
<div style="background:#1B3A5C;border-radius:10px;padding:20px 24px;color:#fff;margin-bottom:24px">
 <h3 style="font-size:15px;font-weight:700;color:#fff;margin-bottom:10px">The Core Training Problem</h3>
 <p style="font-size:13px;color:rgba(255,255,255,.85);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:10px">ATHENA's interventions are designed to counteract automatic cognitive processing. Training that is itself absorbed automatically defeats the purpose. An analyst who can recall what the Intention Gate does is not the same as an analyst who genuinely activates their prior belief before reading AI output.</p>
 <p style="font-size:13px;color:rgba(255,255,255,.75);line-height:1.65;margin:0">Feature tutorials produce compliance. Conceptual grounding produces genuine engagement. The sequence is always: <strong style="color:#fff">why the failure mode exists → why the intervention counteracts it → how to use it.</strong> Never the reverse.</p>
</div>

<!-- Design principles -->
<h3 class="subsec">Training Design Principles</h3>
<div class="grid grid-3" style="gap:10px;margin-bottom:24px">
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #1B3A5C;padding:14px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#1B3A5C;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:6px">Principle 1</div>
  <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Why Before What</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">Every intervention is introduced with its cognitive failure mode before its mechanics. The sequence is never "here is the feature, here is when to use it." It is always "here is the failure mode, here is why it occurs in AI-assisted analysis, here is what the platform does to counteract it."</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #0D6B5E;padding:14px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#0D6B5E;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:6px">Principle 2</div>
  <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Practice Over Declarative Knowledge</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">Conceptual understanding without practice does not transfer to operational behavior. Scenario-based exercises with calibration feedback are the most important training element. Modules not followed by structured practice with feedback produce declarative knowledge that does not change behavior.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #9A6800;padding:14px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#9A6800;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:6px">Principle 3</div>
  <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Genuine Engagement vs. Compliance</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">The Intention Gate works only if the prior belief is genuinely committed before reading the response. An analyst who fills it in after reading has undermined the one thing it does. Training must make this concrete with worked examples — not frame the gate as a checkbox requirement.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #6D4FB5;padding:14px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#6D4FB5;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:6px">Principle 4</div>
  <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Calibration-Aware Scaffolding</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">Training intensity scales with calibration state. New analysts receive more scaffolding and more supervised practice. Training does not end at onboarding — it continues as analysts enter new domains where calibration reverts toward PRIOR ONLY, and when model version changes require recalibration.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #8B2222;padding:14px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:6px">Principle 5</div>
  <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Adversarial Context Throughout</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">Training must explain the adversarial exploitation of cognitive failure modes — corpus poisoning, prompt injection, deadline-targeted D&D. Analysts who do not understand the adversarial dimension treat the platform's guardrails as organizational caution rather than operational necessity.</p>
 </div>
 <div class="card" style="border-top:3px solid #0D6B5E;padding:14px">
  <div style="font-size:10px;font-weight:700;color:#0D6B5E;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin-bottom:6px">Principle 6</div>
  <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);margin-bottom:6px">Management Reinforcement Required</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">Training content cannot override management behavior. Supervisors who use gaming detection scores punitively undermine the training message immediately. Supervisory training must precede analyst training rollout, and supervisory behavior must be explicitly aligned with the developmental framing of the calibration system.</p>
 </div>
</div>

<!-- Knowledge domains -->
<h3 class="subsec">Four Knowledge Domains</h3>
<p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin-bottom:16px">The domains are sequenced — Domain 1 is the prerequisite for understanding Domains 2 and 3, and Domain 4 depends on all three.</p>

<div style="display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:12px;margin-bottom:24px">

 <div class="card" style="border-left:5px solid #1B3A5C;padding:16px">
  <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12px;margin-bottom:10px">
   <span style="background:#1B3A5C;color:#fff;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;padding:3px 10px;border-radius:4px">Domain 1</span>
   <span style="font-size:14px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy)">The Fluency Trap</span>
  </div>
  <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:10px">AI output is uniformly fluent regardless of epistemic status. A retrieved claim and a confabulated claim sound identical. This is structural — the generation process treats retrieved text and parametric knowledge as inputs to the same probability distribution. The claim that emerges does not carry a label saying where it came from.</p>
  <div style="display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr;gap:12px">
   <div>
    <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#1B3A5C;margin-bottom:5px">What analysts must understand</div>
    <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.7;margin:0">
     <li>GRND vs. PARAM vs. SYNTH at the conceptual level — before meeting the badges</li>
     <li>Why fluency signals credibility for human speakers but not AI systems</li>
     <li>Why better AI models do not eliminate the problem — confident-sounding errors look identical to accurate outputs</li>
    </ul>
   </div>
   <div>
    <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;margin-bottom:5px">Key misconception to address</div>
    <p style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin:0">"The AI will tell me if it is uncertain." Models produce hedging language that does not reliably correspond to lower accuracy. Calibrated confidence signals in ATHENA are grounded in measured accuracy — not model self-report.</p>
   </div>
  </div>
  <div style="margin-top:10px;padding:8px 12px;background:#EEF2F8;border-radius:5px;font-size:11.5px;color:#1B3A5C"><strong>Learning objective:</strong> Analyst can articulate, without reference to the platform, why AI confidence signals cannot be taken at face value and what they would need to know about a claim to evaluate it properly.</div>
 </div>

 <div class="card" style="border-left:5px solid #0D6B5E;padding:16px">
  <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12px;margin-bottom:10px">
   <span style="background:#0D6B5E;color:#fff;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;padding:3px 10px;border-radius:4px">Domain 2</span>
   <span style="font-size:14px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy)">The Three Accountability Axes</span>
  </div>
  <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:12px">Every AI-assisted analytical interaction must be accountable across three independent dimensions. Passing two provides no protection against failing the third. The independence is the critical point.</p>
  <div style="display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr 1fr;gap:10px;margin-bottom:12px">
   <div style="background:#EEF2F8;border-radius:6px;padding:10px">
    <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#1B3A5C;margin-bottom:4px">ORIGIN</div>
    <p style="font-size:11px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">The verification path for a retrieved claim is completely different from a parametric claim. An analyst who does not know the source type cannot apply the right verification action.</p>
   </div>
   <div style="background:#E6F5F3;border-radius:6px;padding:10px">
    <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#0D6B5E;margin-bottom:4px">CURRENCY</div>
    <p style="font-size:11px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">The model does not know the intelligence picture has changed. Currency failure is silent — there is no signal in the output that a valid assessment has been superseded.</p>
   </div>
   <div style="background:#FFF7E8;border-radius:6px;padding:10px">
    <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#9A6800;margin-bottom:4px">TRUST</div>
    <p style="font-size:11px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.6;margin:0">Calibration is a relationship between this analyst, this claim type, and this domain. Domain expertise does not transfer to AI calibration. Without measurement, systematic over-reliance is invisible.</p>
   </div>
  </div>
  <div style="padding:8px 12px;background:#E6F5F3;border-radius:5px;font-size:11.5px;color:#0D6B5E"><strong>Learning objective:</strong> Given a specific AI claim, analyst identifies which axis failure modes apply, what the correct verification action is, and what genuine verification looks like for that source type.</div>
 </div>

 <div class="card" style="border-left:5px solid #9A6800;padding:16px">
  <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12px;margin-bottom:10px">
   <span style="background:#9A6800;color:#fff;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;padding:3px 10px;border-radius:4px">Domain 3</span>
   <span style="font-size:14px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy)">Verification as Craft</span>
  </div>
  <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:12px">Verification actions are not checkbox compliance. Different source types have qualitatively different verification paths with different epistemic weight. "Could not verify" is not failure — it is honest accounting. An unverified claim explicitly marked unverified is far less dangerous than one that appears verified.</p>
  <table class="decision-table" style="margin-bottom:12px">
   <tr><th>Source Type</th><th>Verification Path</th><th>Key Distinction</th></tr>
   <tr><td><strong>GRND</strong></td><td>Locate source document, confirm accurate characterization</td><td>Highest FGTS calibration weight</td></tr>
   <tr><td><strong>PARAM</strong></td><td>Independent collection or SME consultation</td><td>No specific source to check — must verify the claim in the world</td></tr>
   <tr><td><strong>SYNTH</strong></td><td>Verify underlying sources support the synthesized conclusion</td><td>Sources existing and being current is not sufficient</td></tr>
   <tr><td><strong>TRANSCRIPT</strong></td><td>Verify against original recording, not the AI transcript</td><td>ASR accuracy varies; badge confidence reflects extraction accuracy</td></tr>
   <tr><td><strong>IMAGE/VIDEO/AUDIO</strong></td><td>Check MAS/EIDOLON authenticity assessment first</td><td>High deepfake risk creates hard confidence ceiling</td></tr>
   <tr><td><strong>OCR</strong></td><td>Return to original document if confidence score is low</td><td>Badge score reflects text extraction accuracy, not claim accuracy</td></tr>
  </table>
  <div style="padding:8px 12px;background:#FFF7E8;border-radius:5px;font-size:11.5px;color:#9A6800"><strong>Learning objective:</strong> For each source type badge, analyst explains the correct verification path and what genuine verification looks like in practice — not just the correct button label.</div>
 </div>

 <div class="card" style="border-left:5px solid #6D4FB5;padding:16px">
  <div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12px;margin-bottom:10px">
   <span style="background:#6D4FB5;color:#fff;font-size:11px;font-weight:700;padding:3px 10px;border-radius:4px">Domain 4</span>
   <span style="font-size:14px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy)">Calibration Literacy</span>
  </div>
  <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:12px">Calibration is not a score the platform assigns — it is a continuously updated model of the relationship between this analyst's reliance on AI and the AI's actual accuracy on this claim type in this domain with this model version. It changes as any variable changes.</p>
  <div style="display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr;gap:12px;margin-bottom:12px">
   <div>
    <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#6D4FB5;margin-bottom:6px">Calibration State Machine</div>
    <div style="display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:6px">
     <div style="background:#EEF2F8;border-radius:5px;padding:8px 10px;font-size:11px">
      <strong style="color:#1B3A5C">PRIOR ONLY:</strong> Initialized from benchmarks. Reflects general model accuracy, not this analyst's verified domain accuracy. Treat with explicit humility.
     </div>
     <div style="background:#FFF7E8;border-radius:5px;padding:8px 10px;font-size:11px">
      <strong style="color:#9A6800">CALIBRATING:</strong> Operational data accumulating. Posterior updating but not yet stable. Signals improving but carry higher uncertainty.
     </div>
     <div style="background:#E6F5F3;border-radius:5px;padding:8px 10px;font-size:11px">
      <strong style="color:#0D6B5E">CALIBRATED:</strong> Empirically grounded for this analyst, claim type, domain, model version. Confidence signals reliable within calibration model bounds.
     </div>
    </div>
   </div>
   <div>
    <div style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;margin-bottom:6px">What Degrades Calibration</div>
    <ul style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub);padding-left:14px;line-height:1.7;margin:0">
     <li>Model version change resets to PRIOR ONLY for affected claim types</li>
     <li>Domain change requires domain-specific recalibration</li>
     <li>Compliance gaming — performative verification builds an inaccurate record, producing unreliable confidence signals the analyst then relies on</li>
    </ul>
    <div style="margin-top:10px;padding:8px 10px;background:#FBF0F0;border-radius:5px;font-size:11px;color:#6B1818"><strong>Gaming is self-defeating:</strong> Analysts who game the verification system receive worse confidence signals. Their own analytical outputs are degraded by signals they cannot trust.</div>
   </div>
  </div>
  <div style="padding:8px 12px;background:#EDE8FF;border-radius:5px;font-size:11.5px;color:#4A2FA8"><strong>Learning objective:</strong> Analyst explains their current calibration state in their primary domain, what last changed it, and which domains remain in CALIBRATING or PRIOR ONLY.</div>
 </div>

</div>

<!-- Curriculum architecture -->
<h3 class="subsec">Curriculum Architecture</h3>
<table class="decision-table" style="margin-bottom:24px">
 <tr><th>Module</th><th>Target</th><th>Duration</th><th>Format</th><th>Prerequisite</th></tr>
 <tr>
  <td><strong>Module 0</strong><br><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">Foundation (Pre-Platform)</span></td>
  <td>All analysts — before any platform access</td>
  <td>Half day</td>
  <td>Facilitated session with case studies. No platform demonstrated.</td>
  <td>None — this is the prerequisite for everything else</td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td><strong>Module 1</strong><br><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">ATHENA Orientation</span></td>
  <td>All analysts</td>
  <td>Half day</td>
  <td>Structured walkthrough with hands-on exercises on historical cases</td>
  <td>Module 0</td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td><strong>Module 2</strong><br><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">Pressure Mode Operations</span></td>
  <td>All analysts</td>
  <td>Half day</td>
  <td>Tabletop exercise with <strong>mandatory live pressure simulation</strong></td>
  <td>Module 1</td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td><strong>Module 3</strong><br><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">Advanced Calibration</span></td>
  <td>Analysts with 30+ days operational ATHENA use</td>
  <td>Half day</td>
  <td>Individual calibration review session with supervisor. <strong>Recurring quarterly.</strong></td>
  <td>Module 1 + 30 days operational</td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td><strong>Module 4</strong><br><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">Supervisory Operations</span></td>
  <td>Analytical supervisors</td>
  <td>Full day</td>
  <td>Facilitated workshop</td>
  <td>Modules 0 and 1</td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td><strong>Module 5</strong><br><span style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted)">IOB Operations</span></td>
  <td>IOB members, program managers</td>
  <td>Half day</td>
  <td>Facilitated briefing</td>
  <td>None</td>
 </tr>
</table>

<!-- Hard training problems -->
<h3 class="subsec">Three Hard Training Problems</h3>
<div style="display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:12px;margin-bottom:24px">

 <div class="card" style="border-left:5px solid #8B2222;padding:16px">
  <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:#8B2222;margin-bottom:8px">1 — Pressure Mode Resistance</div>
  <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:10px">Deadline-Critical constraints will feel obstructive to analysts under genuine operational pressure. Training must address this directly rather than presenting the friction as seamless.</p>
  <div style="display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr 1fr;gap:10px">
   <div style="background:#FBF0F0;border-radius:5px;padding:10px;font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub)"><strong style="color:#8B2222;display:block;margin-bottom:4px">Cognitive prep</strong>The asymmetric cost argument must be established before the simulation: the cost of a miscalibrated high-stakes assessment under deadline pressure exceeds the cost of the constraints. State this explicitly. Do not present the constraints as seamless.</div>
   <div style="background:#FBF0F0;border-radius:5px;padding:10px;font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub)"><strong style="color:#8B2222;display:block;margin-bottom:4px">Live simulation</strong>Not optional. Conceptual understanding of deadline pressure dynamics does not transfer to operational behavior without an experience that activates the pressure response. The debrief consolidates understanding from a concrete starting point.</div>
   <div style="background:#FBF0F0;border-radius:5px;padding:10px;font-size:11.5px;color:var(--sub)"><strong style="color:#8B2222;display:block;margin-bottom:4px">Management alignment</strong>Supervisors who pressure analysts to produce quickly in ways that implicitly endorse circumventing DC constraints undermine this module immediately. Supervisory training must address this directly.</div>
  </div>
 </div>

 <div class="card" style="border-left:5px solid #9A6800;padding:16px">
  <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:#9A6800;margin-bottom:8px">2 — Intention Gate: Genuine vs. Performative</div>
  <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:8px">Analysts briefed on compliance gaming detection may approach the Intention Gate strategically — writing substantive-sounding entries while still filling them in after reading the AI response.</p>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin:0">Training must say directly: "You cannot outrun the calibration model. Over time, genuine pre-commitment and post-hoc simulation produce different calibration records." More importantly: the training goal is not "write convincing Intention Gate entries." It is "understand that the gate only counteracts anchoring if the prior belief is committed before the AI output sets the anchor." The entry is a byproduct of genuine pre-commitment, not the thing itself.</p>
 </div>

 <div class="card" style="border-left:5px solid #6D4FB5;padding:16px">
  <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:#6D4FB5;margin-bottom:8px">3 — Gaming Detection: Developmental, Not Punitive</div>
  <p style="font-size:12.5px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:8px">The compliance gaming score being visible to supervisors will feel adversarial. Training content should say directly:</p>
  <div style="background:#F5F0FF;border:1px solid #C4A8F0;border-radius:6px;padding:12px 14px;font-size:12px;color:#3A1A70;line-height:1.65;margin-bottom:8px">"Some of you will feel this makes ATHENA a surveillance system. Here is what it actually is: calibration is only useful if it reflects genuine verification behavior. An analyst whose calibration is built on gaming behavior gets worse confidence signals, which produces worse analytical outcomes for them. The score exists to help you and your supervisor understand whether your calibration data is reliable — and to protect you from acting on signals that do not reflect verified accuracy."</div>
  <p style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.65;margin:0">Supervisors must then behave consistently with this framing. If the first mention of a gaming detection score is in a performance review, the training message is undermined. The first mention should be in a calibration development conversation.</p>
 </div>

</div>

<!-- Development priorities -->
<h3 class="subsec">Development Priorities</h3>
<table class="decision-table" style="margin-bottom:12px">
 <tr><th>Priority</th><th>Module</th><th>Rationale</th><th>Must precede</th></tr>
 <tr><td><strong style="color:#8B2222">P1</strong></td><td>Module 0 — Foundation</td><td>Prerequisite for everything. Without it all other training produces compliance at best.</td><td>All other modules</td></tr>
 <tr><td><strong style="color:#8B2222">P2</strong></td><td>Module 4 — Supervisory</td><td>Supervisors must be trained before analysts. Their behavior shapes whether training takes root or is undermined.</td><td>Module 3 rollout</td></tr>
 <tr><td><strong style="color:#9A6800">P3</strong></td><td>Module 1 — Orientation + onboarding calibration session</td><td>Analysts cannot begin operational use without this. Onboarding calibration provides cold-start data.</td><td>Operational ATHENA use</td></tr>
 <tr><td><strong style="color:#9A6800">P4</strong></td><td>Module 2 — Pressure Mode</td><td>Must be completed before analysts encounter any high-stakes time-constrained requirements in production.</td><td>High-stakes operational use</td></tr>
 <tr><td><strong style="color:#0D6B5E">P5</strong></td><td>Module 3 — Advanced Calibration</td><td>Requires 30+ days operational data. Can be developed in parallel with Phase 1-2 platform deployment.</td><td>—</td></tr>
 <tr><td><strong style="color:#0D6B5E">P6</strong></td><td>Module 5 — IOB Operations</td><td>Not required until accountability surface is operational (Phase 3-4).</td><td>—</td></tr>
</table>

</div>

<div class="section-title">Research References</div>

<div class="card" style="border-left:4px solid var(--teal);margin-bottom:24px;padding:14px 18px">
 <h4 style="color:var(--teal-dark)">About These References</h4>
 <p style="margin-top:6px;font-size:12.5px;line-height:1.65">The ATHENA intervention design is grounded in peer-reviewed human factors, cognitive science, and HCI research. The THEMIS architecture draws on established software engineering patterns and legal technology governance frameworks. References are organized by domain. Each entry includes a note on how the research informed specific design decisions — the mapping between theory and implementation is explicit, not assumed. The Transfer Assumption (that findings from lab contexts transfer to high-stakes professional intelligence analysis) is noted as a gap requiring empirical validation by the Research and Red Team through the pre-registered research cyc

  actions:['Figure confirmed — analyst locates the specific figure or conclusion in a primary source. document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded',()=>{
 try {
const el=document.getElementById('badge-detail');
 BADGES.forEach(b=>{
  el.innerHTML+=`<details class="iv-card" style="margin-bottom:10px">
   <summary style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12px;padding:12px 16px;cursor:pointer;background:#fff;list-style:none">
    <span class="src-badge" style="color:${b.col};border-color:${b.col};font-size:12px;padding:3px 9px;flex-shrink:0">${b.code}</span>
    <span style="font-size:13px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy);flex:1">${b.label}</span>
    <span style="font-size:10px;color:var(--muted);margin-left:auto">▸</span>
   </summary>
   <div class="iv-body" style="display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr;gap:16px">
    <div>
     <h5>Trigger Condition</h5><p>${b.trigger}</p>
     <h5>Confidence Ceiling</h5><p>${b.ceiling}</p>
     <h5>Failure Mode Addressed</h5><p>${b.failure}</p>
    </div>
    <div>
     <h5>Verification Action Set</h5>
     <ul>${b.actions.map(a=>`<li>${a}</li>`).join('')}</ul>
     <h5>THEMIS Integration</h5><p>${b.themis}</p>
    </div>
   </div>
  </details>`;});
 // fix summary arrows on open
 document.querySelectorAll('#badge-detail details').forEach(d=>{
  d.addEventListener('toggle',()=>{
   const arr=d.querySelector('summary span:last-child');
   if(arr) arr.textContent=d.open?'▾':'▸';
  });
 });
 } catch(e) { console.error('THEMIS render error [2]:', e.message, e.stack); }
});

Elevating IAS/SCUDO during DC mode is a D&D countermeasure, not only a calibration measure.','Hard stops vs. soft nudges: the deliberate choice to make these constraints non-dismissable reflects the asymmetric cost of failure under deadline pressure. Soft nudges are insufficient when deadline pressure maximally degrades deliberate processing.'],svcs:['PGS/NOMOS','Provenance/MOIRAI','TCS/MIMIR','FGTS/ALETHEIA']},
];

const CATS=[
 {name:'Session Configuration',cc:'#2E5FAD',cl:'#EEF3FF'},
 {name:'Session Scaffolding',cc:'#6D4FB5',cl:'#F2EEFD'},
 {name:'Pre-Response Interventions',cc:'#9A6800',cl:'#FFF7E8'},
 {name:'Response Interventions',cc:'#0D6B5E',cl:'#E6F5F3'},
 {name:'Verification Infrastructure',cc:'#0D6B5E',cl:'#E6F5F3'},
 {name:'Behavioral Telemetry',cc:'#1B3A5C',cl:'#EEF2F8'},
 {name:'Session Integrity',cc:'#8B2222',cl:'#FBF0F0'},
 {name:'Pressure Response',cc:'#6D4FB5',cl:'#F2EEFD'},
];
document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded',()=>{
let el;
 try {
el=document.getElementById('iv-catalog');
 if(!el){throw new Error('iv-catalog element missing');}
CATS.forEach(cat=>{
  const ivs=IVDATA.filter(iv=>iv.cat===cat.name);
  if(!ivs.length)return;
  const section=document.createElement('div');
  section.className='cat-group';
  section.innerHTML=`<div class="cat-label" style="background:${cat.cc};color:#fff">${cat.name} · ${ivs.length} intervention${ivs.length>1?'s':''}</div>`;
  ivs.sort((a,b)=>a.n-b.n).forEach(iv=>{
   const d=document.createElement('details');
   d.className='iv-card';
   d.innerHTML=`<summary>
    <span class="iv-num">${iv.n}</span>
    <span class="iv-name">${iv.name}</span>
    <span class="iv-cat-pill" style="background:${iv.cl};color:${iv.cc}">${iv.cat}</span>
   </summary>
   <div class="iv-body">
    <h5>Purpose</h5><p>${iv.purpose}</p>
    <h5>Key Mechanisms</h5><ul>${iv.mechs.map(m=>`<li>${m}</li>`).join('')}</ul>
    <h5>THEMIS Integration</h5>
    <div class="svc-pills">${iv.svcs.map(s=>`<span class="svc-pill">${s}</span>`).join('')}</div>
   </div>`;
   section.appendChild(d);});
  el.appendChild(section);
 });
 } catch(e) { console.error('Handler 3 error:',e.message); if(el) el.innerHTML='<div style="color:red;padding:16px;font-family:monospace;font-size:12px"><b>Interventions render error:</b> '+e.message+'</div>'; }
});

const IV_NAMES={};
IVDATA.forEach(iv=>IV_NAMES[iv.n]=iv.name);

document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded',()=>{
let tbl;
 try {
tbl=document.getElementById('mx-table');
 if(!tbl){throw new Error('mx-table element missing');}
const nCore=SVCS_CORE.length, nAdd=SVCS_ADD.length, nIntel=SVCS_INTEL.length;
 const totalCols=nCore+nAdd+nIntel;
 const counts={P:new Array(totalCols).fill(0),C:new Array(totalCols).fill(0),B:new Array(totalCols).fill(0)};

 // Build header with group separators and colour coding
 let header=`<thead><tr><th rowspan="2" style="vertical-align:bottom;min-width:200px">Intervention</th>`;
 header+=`<th colspan="${nCore}" style="background:#1B3A5C;border-right:3px solid #000;font-size:10px;padding:4px 0;text-align:center">Original 9 — THEMIS Platform Services</th>`;
 header+=`<th colspan="${nAdd}" style="background:#0D6B5E;border-right:3px solid #000;font-size:10px;padding:4px 0;text-align:center">Addendum A — 5 New Services</th>`;
 header+=`<th colspan="${nIntel}" style="background:#4A2FA8;font-size:10px;padding:4px 0;text-align:center">Intelligence Layer — Year 2-3</th>`;
 header+=`</tr><tr>`;
 SVCS_CORE.forEach((s,i)=>header+=`<th title="${SVCS_CORE_FULL[i]}" style="background:#1B3A5C${i===nCore-1?';border-right:3px solid #000':''}">${s}</th>`);
 SVCS_ADD.forEach((s,i)=>header+=`<th title="${SVCS_ADD_FULL[i]}" style="background:#0D7060${i===nAdd-1?';border-right:3px solid #000':''}">${s}</th>`);
 SVCS_INTEL.forEach((s,i)=>header+=`<th title="${SVCS_INTEL_FULL[i]}" style="background:#5A3DBF;font-size:10px">${s}</th>`);
 header+=`</tr></thead><tbody>`;

 const seen=new Set();
 let body='';
 MX_CATS.forEach(cat=>{
  const unique=cat.ivs.filter(n=>!seen.has(n));
  unique.forEach(n=>seen.add(n));
  if(!unique.length)return;
  body+=`<tr class="mx-cat"><td colspan="${totalCols+1}" style="border-left:4px solid ${cat.cc}">${cat.name}</td></tr>`;
  unique.forEach(n=>{
   const core=MX[n]||[];
   const ext=MX_EXT[n]||[];
   const all=[...core,...ext];
   body+=`<tr><td><span style="color:${cat.cc};font-weight:700;margin-right:6px">${n}</span>${IV_NAMES[n]||''}</td>`;
   all.forEach((v,i)=>{
    const borderStyle=i===nCore-1||i===nCore+nAdd-1?';border-right:3px solid #ccc':'';
    if(v==='P'){counts.P[i]++;body+=`<td class="mx-p"${borderStyle?` style="${borderStyle.slice(1)}"`:''}>P</td>`;}
    else if(v==='C'){counts.C[i]++;body+=`<td class="mx-c"${borderStyle?` style="${borderStyle.slice(1)}"`:''}>C</td>`;}
    else if(v==='B'){counts.B[i]++;counts.P[i]++;counts.C[i]++;body+=`<td class="mx-b"${borderStyle?` style="${borderStyle.slice(1)}"`:''}>B</td>`;}
    else body+=`<td class="mx-n"${borderStyle?` style="${borderStyle.slice(1)}"`:''}>—</td>`;});
   body+='</tr>';
  });
 });
 // Totals row
 body+=`<tr style="background:var(--bg);font-weight:600;border-top:2px solid var(--border)"><td style="font-size:11px;color:var(--muted);font-weight:700">TOTAL</td>`;
 for(let i=0;i<totalCols;i++){
  const p=counts.P[i],c=counts.C[i];
  const border=i===nCore-1||i===nCore+nAdd-1?'border-right:3px solid #ccc;':'';
  body+=`<td style="background:var(--bg);padding:5px 8px;${border}"><div style="font-size:9px;color:#1E40AF;font-weight:700">P:${p}</div><div style="font-size:9px;color:#065F46;font-weight:700">C:${c}</div></td>`;
 }
 body+='</tr></tbody>';
 tbl.innerHTML=header+body;
 } catch(e) { console.error('Handler 4 error:',e.message); if(tbl) tbl.innerHTML='<caption style="color:red;font-family:monospace;font-size:12px"><b>Matrix render error:</b> '+e.message+'</caption>'; }
});


const REFS={
 'Human Factors and Automation':[
  {auth:'Parasuraman, R., & Manzey, D. H.',year:'2010',title:'Complacency and Bias in Human Use of Automation: An Attentional Integration',venue:'Human Factors, 52(3), 381–410',note:'Primary empirical basis for automation complacency and bias claims. Documents both omission and commission errors under imperfect automation. Key finding: expertise does not protect against automation bias. Informed the core justification for ATHENA\'s intervention architecture.'},
  {auth:'Mackworth, N. H.',year:'1948',title:'The Breakdown of Vigilance During Prolonged Visual Search',venue:'Quarterly Journal of Experimental Psychology, 1(1), 6–21',note:'Radar operator vigilance study. Documents 10–15% signal detection accuracy degradation within the first 30 minutes of a monitoring task, continuing across a two-hour session. Foundation for the Episode Boundary intervention design and the vigilance decrement countermeasure rationale.'},
  {auth:'Lee, J. D., & See, K. A.',year:'2004',title:'Trust in Automation: Designing for Appropriate Reliance',venue:'Human Factors, 46(1), 50–80',note:'Defines the normative target for trust calibration: trust that accurately tracks actual system reliability, not maximum trust or maximum scepticism. Provides the theoretical basis for the Trust Calibration Service (TCS/MIMIR) and the Reliance-Accuracy Index (RAI) construct.'},
  {auth:'Parasuraman, R., Sheridan, T. B., & Wickens, C. Informs the design of ATHENA\'s intervention intensity calibration across different interaction classes.'},
 ],
 'Cognitive Psychology and Decision Making':[
  {auth:'Kahneman, D.',year:'2011',title:'Thinking, Fast and Slow',venue:'Farrar, Straus and Giroux',note:'Dual-process theory (System 1/System 2). Foundational framing for ATHENA\'s intervention design: interventions are designed to activate System 2 deliberative processing in contexts where System 1 fluency-based processing would otherwise dominate. Anchoring effects and their resistance to correction directly motivate the Intention Gate design.'},
  {auth:'Lerner, J. S., & Tetlock, P. E.',year:'1999',title:'Accounting for the Effects of Accountability',venue:'Psychological Bulletin, 125(2), 255–275',note:'Accountability and evaluation apprehension as mechanisms for improving decision quality. Basis for the Agreement Rate Tracker (social accountability through visible pattern), the Compliance Gaming Detection supervisory visibility dimension, and the cross-analyst disagreement detection design rationale.'},
  {auth:'Craik, F. I. M., & Lockhart, R. Primary basis for the Reformulation Gate intervention — writing a paraphrase forces semantic engagement that surface reading does not.'},
  {auth:'Tversky, A., & Kahneman, D.',year:'1974',title:'Judgment under Uncertainty: Heuristics and Biases',venue:'Science, 185(4157), 1124–1131',note:'Anchoring, availability, and representativeness heuristics. The anchoring heuristic directly motivates the pre-response intervention design (Intention Gate, Input Type Classification) — the model always speaks first in a standard interface and anchors all subsequent reasoning.'},
  {auth:'Nickerson, R. Tests three cognitive forcing functions against a control condition.  'Memory and Prospective Cognition':[

Primary basis for the Intention Gate design: setting a verification intention before reading creates a cognitive hook that survives engagement with fluent model output.'},
  {auth:'Zeigarnik, B.',year:'1927',title:'Über das Behalten von erledigten und unerledigten Handlungen',venue:'Psychologische Forschung, 9, 1–85',note:'The Zeigarnik Effect: incomplete tasks persist in memory more strongly than completed ones. Foundational basis for the Verification Queue design (open verification tasks create explicit incomplete tasks rather than implicit responsibility) and the Episode Boundary commitment mechanism.'},
  {auth:'Von Restorff, H.',year:'1933',title:'Über die Wirkung von Bereichsbildungen im Spurenfeld',venue:'Psychologische Forschung, 18, 299–342',note:'The isolation effect (Von Restorff Effect): an item that stands out from its surroundings is more likely to be remembered. Basis for the Claim Confidence Badge design — uncertainty must be structurally visible and visually isolated to be epistemically effective.'},

 'UX and Interface Design Principles':[
  {auth:'Yablonski, J.',year:'2020',title:'Laws of UX: Design Principles for Persuasive, Ethical, and Effective User Interfaces',venue:'O\'Reilly Media. Also available at lawsofux.com',note:'A practitioner-facing synthesis of psychological research most directly relevant to interface design decisions. Multiple laws from this collection directly inform specific ATHENA intervention designs. Von Restorff Effect (isolation effect): visually distinct uncertainty signals receive more attention — primary basis for Claim Confidence Badge design (IV9). Zeigarnik Effect (open tasks persist cognitively): motivates the Verification Queue as an explicit open-task structure (IV13), the Episode Boundary commitment mechanism (IV16), and the Dissemination Gate acknowledgment requirement (IV23). Tesler\'s Law (conservation of complexity): complexity cannot be eliminated, only shifted — motivates surfacing source type and provenance rather than hiding it behind fluent output (IV10). Peak-End Rule (experiences are judged by their peak and end): motivates the Synthesis Workspace as a deliberate cognitive peak before session close (IV22). Miller\'s Law (cognitive capacity ~7 items): informs intervention density and the decision not to require simultaneous engagement with all interventions. Hick\'s Law (decision time increases with number of choices): motivates context-sensitive verification action sets — each source type has a small, specific action set rather than a universal list (IV14). Fitts\'s Law (time to reach target relates to size and distance): motivates the visual prominence and size of verification action buttons relative to the claim they target.'},
  {auth:'Tesler, L.',year:'1980s',title:"Tesler's Law of Conservation of Complexity (informal)",venue:'Xerox PARC; formalized in various HCI literature',note:'Every application has an inherent amount of complexity that cannot be reduced — it can only be shifted between user and system. Directly motivates the Source Type Badge and Context Window Inspector design: the epistemic complexity of AI knowledge sourcing is real and must be made visible rather than hidden.'},
  {auth:'Norman, D.',year:'2013',title:'The Design of Everyday Things (Revised Ed.)',venue:'Basic Books',note:'Affordances, feedback, constraints, and mappings in interface design.   {auth:'Kaur, H., et al.',year:'2020',title:'Interpreting Interpretability: Understanding Data Scientists\' Use of Interpretability Tools for Machine Learning',venue:'CHI 2020',note:'Documents how ML practitioners use interpretability tools in practice — often in post-hoc rationalization rather than genuine understanding.   {auth:'Ouyang, L., Wu, J., Jiang, X., et al.',year:'2022',title:'Training Language Models to Follow Instructions with Human Feedback',venue:'NeurIPS 2022',note:'Foundational paper on RLHF. Documents how human rater preferences shape model behavior. Shows why sycophancy is a structural output: human raters consistently score responses higher when they feel validated, creating a training signal that rewards agreement.'},
  {auth:'Guo, C., Pleiss, G., Sun, Y., & Weinberger, K. Q.',year:'2017',title:'On Calibration of Modern Neural Networks',venue:'ICML 2017',note:'Shows that modern neural networks are often poorly calibrated — they express high confidence on incorrect predictions. Foundation for the requirement that claim confidence badges must derive from external calibration (TCS/MIMIR verification outcomes) rather than model self-report.'},
 ],

Directly informs the ATHENA intervention catalog design and the tradecraft compliance rationale for ERAS/LOGOS and PCES/AEGIS.'},
  {auth:'Office of the Director of National Intelligence',year:'2015',title:'ICD 206: Sourcing Requirements for Disseminated Analytic Products',venue:'Intelligence Community Directive 206',note:'Establishes sourcing requirements for disseminated intelligence products. document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded',()=>{
let el;
 try {
el=document.getElementById('refs-list');
 if(!el){throw new Error('refs-list element missing');}
Object.entries(REFS).forEach(([cat,items])=>{
  const sec=document.createElement('div');
  sec.style.marginBottom='24px';
  sec.innerHTML=`<h3 class="subsec">${cat}</h3>`;
  items.forEach((ref,i)=>{
   sec.innerHTML+=`<div class="card" style="margin-bottom:10px;border-left:4px solid var(--navy)">
    <div style="display:flex;gap:10px;align-items:baseline;margin-bottom:6px">
     <span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:700;color:var(--navy)">${ref.auth}</span>
     <span class="badge" style="background:var(--navy-l);color:var(--navy)">${ref.year}</span>
    </div>
    <div style="font-size:13px;font-weight:600;color:var(--text);margin-bottom:4px;font-style:italic">${ref.title}</div>
    <div style="font-size:11.5px;color:var(--muted);margin-bottom:8px">${ref.venue}</div>
    <div style="font-size:12px;color:var(--sub);line-height:1.55;background:var(--bg);padding:8px 10px;border-radius:4px">${ref.note}</div>
   </div>`;});
  el.appendChild(sec);
 });
 } catch(e) { console.error('Handler 5 error:',e.message); if(el) el.innerHTML='<div style="color:red;padding:16px;font-family:monospace;font-size:12px"><b>References render error:</b> '+e.message+'</div>'; }
});


</script>
</div>
</div>
</body>
</html>
